# Physics: model, numerics, validation

This document is the authoritative description of the physics that is implemented and how. Each section has a status:
- **design:** planned, not yet in code
- **implemented:** in code, with the file and function named
- **validated:** implemented, with the tests that check it and their measured results

Any change to the physics or numerics must update this file in the same commit.

---

## 1. Units — *implemented* (`crates/physics/src/units.rs`)

Internal units are dimensionless. A world is defined by four SI reference values:

| Quantity | Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| length | `L₀` | grid cell size |
| charge | `Q₀` | reference charge |
| mass | `M₀` | reference mass |
| Coulomb constant | `k = 1/(4πε₀)` | set to 1 internally |

Derived units:
- time `T₀ = sqrt(M₀ L₀³ / (k Q₀²))`
- energy `E₀ = k Q₀² / L₀`
- velocity `V₀ = L₀ / T₀`
- the speed of light becomes a dimensionless parameter `c = c_SI / V₀`

`WorldScale` converts between the two. CODATA 2018 constants are used for `k`, `e`, `m_e`, `m_p`; `c` is exact.

In the non-relativistic limit, trajectories depend only on the dimensionless ratio `k q Q / (L E_kin)`. The physical scale enters through `c` (relativity) and, in later stages, through material properties and particle–particle interaction.

## 2. Field sources — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/field.rs`: `Coulomb`, `UniformElectric`)

Fixed charges `Q_i` at positions `r_i`, each a rigid sphere of radius `R_i` with a spherically symmetric charge distribution. Outside the sphere (the only region particles can reach), the shell theorem gives exactly:

```
φ(r) = Σ_i k Q_i / |r − r_i|
E(r) = Σ_i k Q_i (r − r_i) / |r − r_i|³
```

- The sum is evaluated in f64 in a fixed order (the order in the level file). No softening.
- Vector arithmetic uses `glam::DVec3`. Its `dot`, `cross` and `length` are plain multiply/add plus IEEE `sqrt`, with no fused multiply-add (checked in the source; `mul_add` is never called). Results are therefore deterministic across platforms.
- Elementary functions (`sin`, `exp`, `ln`, `pow`, `atan2`, …) on the authoritative path (crates `physics` and `level`) come from the pure-Rust `libm` crate (musl's implementations), which gives the same bits on every platform. The standard library's call the platform's C library, whose results differ in the last bit between Windows and Linux: the golden hashes (T11) never held on Linux CI until this was changed (2026-10-05; the first difference was level 8, an `atan2` in a detector's direction window, and the step-size control's `pow` touches every flight). Clippy enforces it (`clippy.toml`: the standard library's versions are disallowed; the game's pictures and the tests' references opt out).
- `UniformElectric` (potential `−E·x`) exists for tests with analytic solutions (T6).
- Test particles are rigid, spherically symmetric and **non-polarizable** (a stated approximation, since a conducting microsphere would feel image forces). Under these assumptions, the force on a test particle of radius `a` equals `q E(center)` exactly, because the force between two non-overlapping spherically symmetric distributions equals the point-charge force.
- Contact condition: `|x − r_i| ≤ R_i + a`, which means the particle is lost.

### 2.1 Charge clouds (Thomson's atom) — *validated* (`field.rs::ChargeCloud`, tests C1–C8)

A charge cloud is a sphere of uniform charge density (total `Q`, radius `R`) that particles fly through. Inside, Gauss's law gives exactly

```
E = Q d / R³,   φ = Q (3R² − |d|²) / (2R³)      (d = x − centre, |d| < R)
```

and outside it is a point charge. A charge `q` of the opposite sign is bound harmonically inside, with `ω₀² = |qQ| / (m R³)`: Jackson's model of a bound electron (§16.7), realised by electrostatics rather than a spring, so the potential, energy conservation and beams need nothing new. The potential map draws the uniform sphere's potential inside (`potential.wgsl`; until the audit of 2026-09-29 it drew a cloud as a point charge with a solid disk, its turning line 3.20 cells from the centre on level 80 instead of the flight's 2.83). The cloud is fixed (it does not recoil or deform), which is the model's approximation, stated in the game's model notes. Metal spheres and electrodes take a cloud as a point charge at its centre (their image systems see only its field outside itself); the level validation keeps clouds clear of metal and the placement check keeps player plates out of them, so this is exact.

- **Scattering by the bound charge (Jackson §16.8, test C3).** Driven by a plane wave, with radiation reaction, the bound charge settles into Jackson's steady state (16.74) with no other damping (Γ' = 0). His equation of motion (16.73) is the Landau–Lifshitz reduction (his (16.10)), with the constant width `Γ = ω₀²τ`. The extinction cross section, the wave's work per time over its intensity `cE₀²/8π`, is his (16.78): `σ_t = σ_T ω⁴/((ω₀² − ω²)² + ω²Γ²)`. That is Rayleigh's `σ_T (ω/ω₀)⁴` below the resonance, the peak `6πc²/ω₀²` (his 6πƛ₀²) at it and Thomson's `σ_T` above it. Nothing is absorbed, so the flight's radiated (Liénard) energy equals it. Abraham–Lorentz's steady state (`x⃛ → iω³x`: the width τω² in place of Γ) differs by up to ω₀τ in the wings of the line and by (ωτ)² far from it, which is the order either reduction neglects.
- **Energy transfer from a passing charge (Jackson Pr. 13.2–13.3, Brau §5.2.3; test C5).** A charge z passing at speed v and impact parameter b leaves in a bound charge at rest the energy `(q²/2m)|∫E e^{iω₀t} dt|²`: the impulse to a free charge, `2z²q²/(m b² v²)`, times `ξ²K₁(ξ)² + ξ²K₀(ξ)²/γ²`, `ξ = ω₀b/(γv)`. A fast pass is a kick; beyond the adiabatic cut-off b ~ γv/ω₀ the charge follows the field and hands the energy back, and the transfer falls as e^{−2ξ} (the level of Pr. 13.2, arc 9). Summed over the impact parameters it is Bohr's classical energy loss (Brau §7.3.1, test C7), in closed form `(4πz²q²/(mv²)) F(ξ_min)`, `F(ξ) = ξK₀K₁ − (β²/2) ξ² (K₁² − K₀²)`: the cut-off acts as a largest impact parameter 1.123 γv/ω₀.
- **A cluster's collective radiation (Brau §4.3.2 and §10.5, Landau & Lifshitz §75; test C6).** Several electrons in one cloud: a uniform field moves only their centre of mass, which oscillates at exactly ω₀ whatever their repulsion (Kohn's theorem, exact for c = ∞; the cluster's dipole plasmon). At finite c each electron also feels the others' radiation fields, the term `(2/3c³) d⃛` of the cluster's dipole moment, so the mode decays N times faster than one electron while the cluster is small against the wavelength (classical superradiance); at its size, the cooperative decay of N dipoles in phase, `Γ₁ Σⱼ F(k r_ij, θ_ij)`. Their mutual magnetic (Darwin) energy makes the mode heavier by `δ = Σ_{j≠i} q²(1 + cos²θ_ij)/(2mc² r_ij)`, lowering its frequency to `ω₀/√(1 + δ)` and its decay by `(1 + δ)²`. The exact retarded interaction gives all of it, and since 2026-10-05 the quasi-static one too (within 0.6 %): it continues each source's past with the jerk of the change of its fields along its path, which carries the others' `(2/3c³) d⃛`, and with the others' acceleration fields and the damping in that motion (§3.3). Before, it lost the collective damping.
- **The dipole sum rule (Jackson Pr. 16.13, test C4).** `∫₀^∞ σ_t dω = 2π²q²/(mc)` assumes a polarizability that tends to the free charge's `−q²/(mω²)`. The radiating oscillator's tends to `−(q²/mω²)(1 − iωτ)`, whose imaginary part is Thomson's plateau, so `∫σ_t dω` diverges. With the free charge's Thomson scattering subtracted, the rule holds up to the order of the reduction, and exactly: `∫₀^∞ (σ_t − σ_T) dω = (2π²q²/mc)(1 − ω₀²τ²)` (Wolfram, symbolically for any Γ < 2ω₀). Abraham–Lorentz's steady state, whose plateau ends at ω ~ 1/τ, integrates to twice the rule as ω₀τ → 0 (1.99999 at ω₀τ = 1.3e-3): its runaway pole lies in the upper half plane, against the premise of Kramers–Kronig.

## 2.2 Magnetic sources — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/magnetic.rs`, `field.rs::LevelField`)

**Units.** Source strengths are given directly in field units, i.e. already multiplied by `μ₀/4π`:
- a dipole by `μ = μ₀ m / 4π`,
- a coil by `κ = μ₀ I / 4π`.

With `k = 1/(4πε₀) = 1` internally, `μ₀/4π = 1/c²`. Specifying the sources in field units keeps magnets meaningful in Newtonian levels (`c = ∞`), while the SI relations hold exactly. The unit of B is `M₀ / (Q₀ T₀)` (from `F = q v × B`).

**Sources.** All three are exact closed forms:

| Source | Field | Obstacle |
|---|---|---|
| Uniformly magnetized sphere | Outside the sphere, exactly the point-dipole field `B = μ (3(m̂·r̂) r̂ − m̂) / r³` | sphere |
| Circular coil | Simpson et al. (NASA/TM-2001-209961). With `α² = (a−ρ)² + z²`, `β² = (a+ρ)² + z²`, `m = k² = 4aρ/β²`: `B_z = 2κ/(α²β) [(a² − ρ² − z²) E + α² K]`, `B_ρ = 2κ z β / (α² ρ) g(m)`, with `g(m) = (1 − m/2) E − (1 − m) K` | torus (wire) |
| Polygonal coil | Sum of exact segment fields `B = κ (r_a × r_b)(|r_a| + |r_b|) / (|r_a| |r_b| (|r_a| |r_b| + r_a·r_b))` | capsules (wires) |

**Circular-coil numerics.**
- `K` and `E` come from the arithmetic–geometric mean (DLMF 19.8), converging quadratically to full precision.
- `1 − m = α²/β²` is passed separately, which avoids cancellation near the wire.
- `g(m)` is `O(m²)`. Evaluated directly it cancels catastrophically near the axis: the first version gave an 85 % error at a point 10⁻¹⁶ off the axis. For `m < 0.05` it is therefore summed as a power series in `m` (DLMF 19.5.1–2), whose `m⁰` and `m¹` terms cancel analytically.

**The 2D slice.** Charges, dipoles perpendicular to the plane (`m ∥ ẑ`) and coils lying in the plane give, at `z = 0`:
- **E** exactly in the plane,
- **B** exactly along `ẑ`, with the in-plane components exactly 0, not just small.

So `q v × B` keeps the particle in the plane (test M5). A straight wire perpendicular to the plane would make B in-plane and push the particle out; such wires are therefore for 3D levels only.

**Energy.** B does no work, so the conserved energy `W` of §4 is unchanged. The forbidden-region map stays exact with magnets.

### Ramped coils and induction — *validated* (`magnetic.rs`: `unit_vector_potential`, `induced_e`; tests M8–M11)

A coil's current may rise linearly, `κ(t) = κ + rate·t` (lab time). Its field scales with κ(t), and it induces the electric field `E = −∂A/∂t = −rate · A_unit(x)`, constant in time for a linear ramp.

- Vector potentials in closed form. Circular loop (Jackson §5.5): `A_φ = (4κ/k) √(a/ρ) [(1 − k²/2) K − E] = 2κ β h(m)/ρ` with `m = k² = 4aρ/β²`, `β² = (a + ρ)² + z²` and `h = (1 − m/2) K − E`, summed as a power series for m < 0.05 where the direct form cancels (`h = π m²/32 + …`). Straight segment: `A = κ û ln((|rb| + rb·û)/(|ra| + ra·û))`, switched to the equal form `(|ra| − ra·û)/(|rb| − rb·û)` behind the segment's start, where the first cancels.
- **Quasi-static**, a stated approximation: the coil's own retardation and radiation are left out (valid while the light time across the setup is short against the time over which the current changes, κ/rate; the model notes give this ratio per level). The metal model cannot screen a non-conservative field, so ramped coils are not combined with metal; nor with magnetic moments (time-dependent B).
- Coils with rate 0 take exactly the previous code path (golden hashes unchanged).

| # | Test | Reference | Criterion | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M8 | Curl of the vector potential of a loop and a polygon coil at 5 points (in and off the plane), central differences h = 1e-5 | the field | < 1e-8 | ≤ 2.4e-10 |
| M9 | Faraday's law (Jackson §5.15): ∮E·dl of the induced field around circles inside a loop (concentric, off-centre) and a polygon coil | −dΦ/dt with Φ the field's flux by quadrature (40 Gauss–Legendre × 256) | < 1e-8 | ≤ 1.5e-14 |
| M10 | A charge in a ramped loop's field (axially symmetric), 60 time units | canonical angular momentum x p_y − y p_x + q (x A_y − y A_x) conserved | < 1e-10 | 1.4e-13 (while \|p\| changes by 47 %) |
| M11 | Jackson §12.5: a charge gyrating (gyroradius 0.5, ω_c ≈ 1) with its guiding centre 2 from the axis of a loop of radius 20; the current rises 4× over 150, 300, 600, 1200 | magnetic moment p²/B(guiding centre), averaged over 3 gyroperiods, conserved (adiabatic invariant); the guiding centre keeps κ(t)Φ(ρ_gc), Φ the loop's exact flux (the canonical angular momentum, exact by symmetry, averages to (q/2π)Φ − (q/2)Br_g², and Br_g² is the invariant): adiabatic end radius 1.0014136664697759 (`scripts/wolfram/m11_adiabatic_flux.wls`, Wolfram Engine 14.2; the uniform-field 2/√4 = 1 plus the field's 0.76 % non-uniformity) | μ: 2e-3; ρ approaches the adiabatic radius as 1/T (the residual halves, ratio 1.7–2.3, per doubled ramp) and within 5e-4 at 1200 | μ: 1.1e-3 … 4e-5; residuals 2.45e-3, 1.36e-3, 6.7e-4, 3.3e-4, ratios 1.80, 2.03, 2.04. (The first version asked ρ within 1 % of 1, the uniform-field value.) |

## 2.3 External fields: stray fields and plane waves — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/external.rs`)

Fields whose sources are outside the arena (disturbances, SPEC §3). `LevelField::external` adds them to the level's own sources. Both kinds are exact solutions of the vacuum Maxwell equations, so they add no approximation:

- **Uniform stray fields** `E` (in the plane) and `B_z`. They are static, and the electric part has potential `−E·x`.
- **Plane waves** travelling in the plane, linearly polarized in the plane:
  ```
  E = E₀ ê cos(ω (t − k̂·x / c) + φ),   B = k̂ × E / c,   ê = ẑ × k̂
  ```
  - `B` is along `ẑ` and `E` lies in the plane, so the 2D slice stays exact (test W3).
  - `k̂·x/c` is multiplied by `ω`, so for `c = ∞` the wave becomes a spatially uniform field oscillating in time with `B = 0`. This is the exact Newtonian limit, used for "mains hum" levels.
  - `ω = 0` gives a static uniform electric field `E₀ ê cos φ`, with its potential `−E·x`, and no magnetic field: the wave vector `k = ω k̂/c` vanishes, so Faraday's law (`k × E = ω B`) no longer ties a `B` to it, and `k̂` only names the direction `ê` is perpendicular to. (Until 2026-10 the code kept `B = k̂ × E / c` there: also a static solution of Maxwell's equations, but not the uniform electric field that the level format and the interface call a static field. No shipped wave has `ω = 0`.) Its vector potential in the gauge `φ = 0` is `−E t` (`PlaneWave::vector_potential`, which divides by ω otherwise).
- A wave travelling out of the plane (k̂ = ±ẑ) with `E` in the plane would have `B` in the plane and push particles out of it. It is therefore not offered in 2D levels.

**Unit consistency.** `B = k̂ × E / c` follows from Faraday's law `∇×E = −∂B/∂t`. That law has this form in every unit system with force `q(E + v×B)`, which is the one used here (§2.2). A unit test checks all four vacuum Maxwell equations for the implemented wave by central differences: `∇·E = ∇·B = 0`, `∇×E = −∂B/∂t`, `∇×B = ∂E/∂t / c²`.

**Levels.** A level lists `disturbances`, each one realization: uniform `E`, `B_z`, and any number of waves. Every shot is flown under every disturbance, and a setup solves the level only if every flight arrives, verified.
- A "random" disturbance, such as the phase of mains hum, is therefore represented by a finite, fixed set of realizations. The game stays deterministic, and every flight is verified individually.
- Robustness between the listed realizations (for example at intermediate phases) is not guaranteed. It is a design parameter of the level (how many realizations it lists).

**Diagnostics.** In time-dependent fields the energy of §4 is not conserved (the wave does work). The energy diagnostic is then reported as NaN rather than a misleading number (`FieldSolver::is_static`). Validation instead uses the exact invariants of motion in a plane wave (test W2).

## 2.4 Antennas: oscillating electric dipoles — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/antenna.rs`)

A small antenna is modelled as a point electric dipole `p(t) = p₀ cos(ωt + φ)` inside a solid sphere (an obstacle). Its fields are the exact retarded fields (Jackson §9.2), at `t_r = t − r/c`, with `n` the unit vector from the dipole and units `k = 1`, `μ₀/4π = 1/c²`:

```
E = [3n(n·p) − p]/r³ + [3n(n·ṗ) − ṗ]/(c r²) + [n × (n × p̈)]/(c² r)
B = (1/c²) [ṗ × n / r² + p̈ × n / (c r)]
```

- This is an exact solution of the vacuum Maxwell equations outside the source point, covering the near (quasi-static), induction and radiation zones. Nothing is truncated.
- For `c = ∞` it becomes the electrostatic dipole field of the instantaneous `p(t)`, with `B = 0`.
- **Potentials** (Lorenz gauge): `φ = n·[p]/r² + n·[ṗ]/(c r)`, `A = [ṗ]/(c² r)`, retarded (`n·p(t)/r²` and 0 for `c = ∞`); `OscillatingDipole::fields` returns this `φ`, `vector_potential` the `A`. Unit test: `E = −∇φ − ∂A/∂t` and `B = ∇ × A` by central differences, oscillating and static, finite and infinite c. A static antenna (`ω = 0`) is an electrostatic dipole with potential `n·p/r²`, which the level field's potential includes (that potential is the static sources' one: an oscillating antenna adds none, like a wave), so `T + qφ` is the conserved energy of its flights (test A4) and the potential map draws it. Until the audit of 2026-09-29 `φ` was stored as 0: a flight past a static antenna reported half its launch energy as an energy error.
- A dipole in the plane (`p ⊥ ẑ`) gives, at `z = 0`, `E` exactly in the plane and `B` exactly along `ẑ`, so the 2D slice stays exact (test A3).
- The radiated power is `⟨P⟩ = p₀² ω⁴ / (3c³)` (Larmor). It is supplied by the generator that drives the antenna, which is outside the model.

**Levels.** Antennas run at the level's RF generator frequency `physics.rf_omega`, or at their own `omega` if they have one. Levels may offer the player a list of frequencies (`limits.antenna_omegas`). All antennas start in phase at t = 0 (φ = 0). An antenna element has amplitude `value` (a negative value is the opposite phase) and orientation `angle_deg`. The player can choose orientations of 0°, 45°, 90° and 135°.
- A shot may have a launch time `t₀` (`launch.time`). The flight then sees the time-dependent sources at `t₀ + t`, implemented as `LevelField::time_offset`, which is exact.
- Identical particles launched at different times follow different trajectories only in time-dependent fields. This is the basis of the RF levels.

## 2.5 The particle's own field: Liénard–Wiechert — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/lienard.rs`)

The exact field of a point charge on an arbitrary world line (Jackson §14.1). With `R = x − r(t_r)`, `n = R/|R|`, `β = v/c`, `κ = 1 − n·β` at the retarded time `t_r`, where `|x − r(t_r)| = c (t − t_r)`:

```
E = q (n − β)(1 − β²) / (κ³ R²)  +  (q/c) n × ((n − β) × β̇) / (κ³ R),     B = n × E / c
```

- The first term is the velocity (generalized Coulomb) field; the second, proportional to the acceleration, is the radiation field.
- The retarded time is the root of `g(t_r) = c(t − t_r) − |x − r(t_r)|`, which is strictly decreasing for `|v| < c`. It is found by bracketing and bisection to full precision.
- For a computed flight the world line is interpolated between the preview's dense-output points: cubic Hermite for position, linear for velocity and acceleration (the acceleration comes from the Lorentz force). Before launch and after the end, the particle is continued with uniform motion. A real launch or stop would itself radiate; this is a stated simplification of the view.
- **Use.** Visual only: the "particle field" map (§10). The particle's own field does not act on the particle except through radiation reaction (§3.1), the consistent classical treatment of that self-interaction.

## 2.6 Conductors: metal spheres — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/conductor.rs`)

Ideal conductors are equipotentials that carry induced charge. They relax in about ε₀/σ ≈ 1e-19 s, which is instantaneous here. The first conductor shape is the sphere. It can be grounded, held at a potential `V` (by a source), or isolated with a net charge `Q` (floating).

**Fixed sources: images plus fundamental solutions.** Induced charges are computed for spheres held at prescribed potentials (Dirichlet systems):
1. **Kelvin images.** A charge `q` at `p`, imaged in a grounded sphere of radius `a` at `c`, gives `−q a/|p − c|` at `c + (p − c) a²/|p − c|²`. The images are imaged again in the other spheres, to depth 7 in preview and 8 in verification. This part carries the singular near field exactly.
2. **Smooth remainder.** The rest is represented by the method of fundamental solutions: `K` equivalent point charges on a shell at 0.6 a inside each sphere (K = 440 in preview, 600 in verification), fitted by least squares to the boundary condition on `2K` surface points (Fibonacci lattices).
3. **Reuse.** The matrix depends only on the geometry. Its Householder QR factorization (own implementation, deterministic, no FMA) is computed once per geometry and cached. New sources, e.g. a moved player charge, only need a new right-hand side.
4. **Bias.**
   - Unit systems `U_j`: sphere `j` at potential 1, the others at 0; seeded by a charge `a_j` at its centre, imaged like a source.
   - Their net charges form the capacitance matrix `C`.
   - The source system `G` (all spheres grounded) plus `Σ α_j U_j` gives sphere `i` the potential `α_i` and net charge `G_i + Σ C_ij α_j`.
   - Grounded: `α = 0`. Fixed potential: `α = V`. Floating: the floating `α` solve the charge equations.
5. **Accuracy.** This is the first model in the game with a finite, non-integration error, so it is measured and made part of verification:
   - The boundary residual, i.e. the largest deviation of the surface potential from its value relative to the sources' potential on the surfaces, is measured on 1000 independent points per sphere. For the Dirichlet part it bounds the potential error everywhere outside (maximum principle). Requirement: < 1e-10 at verification resolution.
   - Preview and verification use different resolutions. So the model error shows up as a preview–verify difference and is compared with the margins like the integration error (§7).

**The particle's image force.** The charges a moving particle induces are:
- its Kelvin images to depth 6 (grounded spheres);
- for floating spheres, a correction with the unit systems and `C⁻¹`. By Green's reciprocity, the charge a unit charge at `x` induces on grounded sphere `j` is `−φ_Uj(x)`. Using it makes the correction `q φ_U(x)ᵀ C⁻¹ φ_U(x)` exactly symmetric.

Truncation by depth (not by size), together with the symmetric correction, keeps the interaction symmetric. The image force `q E_self` is then conservative with potential energy `½ q φ_self(x)`, and the conserved energy of §4 becomes `(γ−1)mc² + qφ + ½ q φ_self`. The neglected deeper images are bounded by `ρ^6`, `ρ` the largest image ratio `a_j/(|c_i − c_j| − a_i)`.

**In levels.**
- `conductors` in the level format. The obstacle is the sphere plus a contact shell of 0.02 cells (`CONTACT_DISTANCE`).
- Player elements cannot be placed inside or against a sphere.
- `Level::model_issues` rejects combining metal with antennas or disturbances, and spheres that touch.
- Verification (`Level::verify_flights`, `physics::verify::verify_pair`) runs the preview flight on the preview conductor model and the tighter flight on the verification model. Their difference enters the verdict.
- Pictures (potential map, field lines, field views) use a coarse display model (60 equivalent charges, images to depth 2, about 1e-4).
- The level test `metal_levels_are_accurate_and_consistent` requires a consistent model and a verification-resolution boundary residual below 1e-10 for each reference placement. The shipped levels have one sphere each, where the image method is exact and the residual is about 1e-15.

**Validity.**
- Electrostatic response: exact for `c = ∞`. For finite `c`, valid while the particle is slow compared with light over the size of the setup.
- A point charge touching a conductor meets an infinite image force. The game rule is therefore that contact happens at a small finite distance (the obstacle is the sphere plus a contact shell).
- Time-dependent sources (antennas, waves) together with conductors would need a full-wave solution and are not combined.
- Magnetic fields: the metal is non-magnetic and the fields are static, so there is no effect.

## 2.7 Electrodes: metal boxes by the boundary element method — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/bem.rs`, `panel.rs`)

Plates, slabs and walls are rectangular metal boxes standing on the plane of the slice. Each is symmetric about z = 0, with an in-plane length and thickness and a finite height. Their surfaces are cut into flat triangles carrying constant surface charge density. The densities follow from collocation at the triangle centroids (surface potential = electrode potential).

- **Exact panel integrals.** The potential and field of a uniformly charged triangle are evaluated in closed form (Wilton et al. 1984; Graglia 1993), for every panel and at every distance. The field is therefore exactly the gradient of the potential of the panel charges, so energy is conserved to integration accuracy. A first version used a 7-point rule for distant panels. Switching between the two broke exact conservation (1.5e-9 drift), and its accuracy at 4 panel sizes (~1e-4) had been overestimated, so it was removed.
- **Symmetry.** Only the upper half is meshed. In the plane, a panel's mirror contributes the same potential and E_z reversed, applied exactly, so E_z = 0 bit for bit.
- **Mesh.** Each face is divided into a grid graded towards the edges (Chebyshev spacing), where the surface density is singular. Panel sizes are 0.5 cells for preview, 0.35 for verification and 1.0 for display.
- **Linear algebra.** Dense LU (own implementation, deterministic), cached per geometry. The bias is handled as for spheres (§2.6): unit systems and the capacitance matrix, with grounded, fixed-potential and floating electrodes.
- **Accuracy.** Constant panels are a discretization. The error is measured (E1, E2) and enters verification through the two mesh resolutions (preview and verification).
- **Particle image force.** Not included for electrodes: it would need a new solve per force evaluation. It scales with q², and levels check that its bound is negligible (§8).
- **Cost.** A flight through a deflector pair (704 panels) takes 16 ms at preview resolution (E5).
- **In levels.** `electrodes` in the level format; the obstacle is the box plus the contact shell (§2.6).
  - `Level::model_issues` rejects combining electrodes with metal spheres (not yet solved together) or with time-dependent sources, and elements or launch points inside an electrode.
  - Verification uses the verification mesh (0.35 cells) against the preview mesh (0.5 cells).
  - Pictures (potential map, field lines, field views) use a display mesh (1 cell). Each panel and its mirror image are integrated exactly where the point is nearer than two of the panel's sizes, and by a three-point rule (degree 2) beyond (`Electrodes::picture`; the potential map's shader does the same per pixel, in f32). Test E6: within 4.3e-6 of the plate potential and 3.5e-5 of the field of that mesh's exact integrals, from 0.05 cells off a plate to 4 cells. The first version took each panel as a point charge at its centroid, up to 4.7e-2 off next to a plate (audit of 2026-09-29), while the legend called the map exact. The display mesh itself differs from the verification mesh by up to 4.2e-3 of the plate potential near the plates (level 19; the preview mesh, the flight's, by 6.9e-4); the legend states it. Field lines over level 19: 0.8 s (2.4 s before the rule's points were precomputed). Never used for physics.
  - The level test `electrode_image_force_is_negligible` bounds the neglected image force by `q²/d²` (four times the force of a flat grounded plane, covering concave corners; `d` floored at 1e-3 cells), relative to `max(|qE|, F₀)` with `F₀ = T₀` per cell (the electric force; magnetic forces would only make the true ratio smaller), along every reference flight, and requires it to be below 1e-10 (`level::IMAGE_FORCE_LIMIT`). The first version used |F_Lorentz| alone, which is meaningless where the force passes through zero (it gave 2.2e-10 in the Einzel lens). Measured on the shipped levels: 1.8e-13 to 1.8e-11 (0 on levels whose flights stay far away).
  - The same bound (`level::electrode_image_force_bound`) is computed along every preview flight in the game and shown in the flight details, with a warning above the limit. The player can steer a particle close to metal, so the reference-flight test alone does not cover the player's setups.
- **Player electrodes.**
  - *Plates* (element kind `plate`): a box of the level's `limits.plate` size centred on a node, along 0° or 90° (any angle in hardcore mode), at a potential set with a slider over the range of `limits.plate_voltages` (0: grounded; the listed values are what the solver tries). They are solved together with the level's electrodes as one BEM system, so moving a plate is a new geometry: a new factorization (about 30 ms for one 4 × 0.4 × 4 plate at preview resolution). The cost meters show it.
  - *Setups not computed* (`Level::setup_issues`, found by the pairwise element check `scripts/pairs.py`): metal spheres touching or overlapping each other or an electrode (the image series and the boundary elements assume separate bodies; such setups failed or ran for minutes), and a particle starting inside or touching metal or on a coil's wire (singular field: NaN forces at t = 0). The game shows the reason instead of flying them; the editor refuses player elements that would create one.
  - *Coil supplies* (element kind `supply` on a `tunable` circular coil's centre, 2026-10-06): set the coil's ramp rate dκ/dt (its κ at t = 0 stays) with a slider over `limits.coil_rates`, or, if a circuit drives it, its source's level, a voltage, with a slider over `limits.supply_voltages` (both symmetric about 0). The supply sits on the coil's terminal, its wire's node due east of the centre (`Coil::supply_node`), so that concentric coils (the betatron's) have their own. The ramp's field and induced field are the existing exact ones (§2.2, test M10). Unit test `coil_supplies_set_the_ramp_or_the_source`.
  - *Power supplies* (element kind `supply`): set the potential of a level electrode marked `tunable`, with a slider over the range of `limits.supply_voltages` (symmetric about 0; the listed values are what the solver tries). Only the bias changes, which reuses the cached factorization (the unit systems are linear in the potentials).
  - Placement rules (`Level::check_placement`): a plate lies entirely inside the grid and the player region; it keeps `PLATE_CLEARANCE` = 1 cell (two preview panels) from every other electrode, so that the surface charge in the gap is resolved; and it stays clear of metal spheres, coil wires, elements, launch points and detectors.
  - Tests: a player plate is bit-identical in field and obstacles to a level electrode with the same geometry and potential, and a supply to a fixed electrode at that potential (`plates_and_supplies_are_electrodes`). Placement rules: `plate_and_supply_placement`.

### Dielectrics — *validated* (tests N1–N5)

Boxes and spheres of relative permittivity ε (`bem::Dielectric`, level fields `dielectrics` and `dielectric_spheres`), meshed as the electrodes and solved in the same system. A dielectric's panels carry its bound charge, fixed by the continuity of the normal displacement: with `λ = (ε − 1)/(ε + 1)` and the average normal field at the surface `Ē`, `σ = (λ/2π) Ē` (k = 1; Jackson §4.4; the panel's own charge contributes no average normal field).

- **Averaged over each panel.** Collocating the condition at the centroid converged slowly near the edges (the cube's polarizability at ε → ∞ was 13 % off at the verification's resolution); averaged over the panel by Dunavant's degree-4 rule (6 points), 5.2 %.
- **Neutrality.** Each body's bound charge sums to zero, which the discrete equation alone does not ensure: the net-charge mode (a free conductor charge) is nearly singular as ε grows, and a nearby charge left −0.6 % (ε = 2) to −48 % (ε = 1e4) of itself on a cube. Each body adds that constraint and one unknown γ, a uniform normal field over its surface (a monopole's field inside, which absorbs the inconsistency): a bordered system.
- **Accuracy (measured, N2–N3).** The cube's polarizability against Helsing & Perfekt (2013): at the verification's resolution (panels 0.35) about 1.1 % for glass (ε = 4), +1.5 % at ε = 0 and −5.2 % at ε → ∞, converging at orders 2.7, 1.5 and 1.3 (the electrodes' first-kind method: 0.75 %, order 2.1). The discretization error enters flights' verification through the two resolutions, as for the electrodes.
- **Spheres.** The upper half of a subdivided octahedron projected onto the sphere (nearly uniform panels, the equator exactly in the mirror plane), scaled so that the polyhedron's volume is the sphere's: flat triangles with their corners on the sphere enclose too little (their sag cost the polarizability 15 % at panels of half the radius, test N4), and the volume is what the polarizability measures first. Their flat facets meet at small kinks, where the normal-field condition converges only at order about 1.2, so spheres get panels of 0.6 of the size (`SPHERE_PANEL`). At the verification's resolution: a glass sphere's polarizability 1.4 % off the exact `3(ε − 1)/(ε + 2)`, ε → ∞ 2.7 %, ε = 0 1.4 %; the field of a charge 0.6 from a glass sphere's surface 1.1 % off the exact Legendre series (N4, N5).
- **Approximations, stated in the game:** static response (no dispersion: the optics stage, `SPEC.md` §8, adds it), no charge the particle induces in them (as for the electrodes, the image force), particles do not enter them, and they keep a contact distance from metal and from each other (`Level::setup_issues`). They respond to the fixed charges only, as the electrodes' surface charge does: levels combining them with metal spheres (whose images do not see them), antennas or outside fields are refused (`Level::model_issues`).

### Ferrites — *validated* (tests F1–F3)

Magnetically soft insulators (`bem::Ferrite`, level field `ferrites`): boxes of relative permeability μ that bend magnetic fields only (a real mu-metal is also a conductor and would screen electric fields too; a ferrite does not). The magnetic problem is the dielectric one with H for E and μ for ε: bound magnetic surface charge `σ = (λ/2π) H̄ₙ`, λ = (μ − 1)/(μ + 1), averaged over each panel as the dielectrics'.

- **Odd under the mirror.** The slice's magnetic sources (in-plane coils, magnets along z) give B along z in the plane, so the bound magnetic charge is odd under z → −z: each panel's mirror carries −σ. In the plane the in-plane components of the ferrite's field then cancel exactly (particles stay in the plane), a body's total magnetic charge is zero by symmetry (no monopoles), and the near-singular net-charge mode the dielectrics needed bordering for does not exist.
- **Accuracy (F1–F2).** The same as the dielectrics' (the cube's and the sphere's polarizability are isotropic, so the references hold for B along z): the cube at μ → ∞ −5.2 % and μ = 0 +1.5 % at the verification's resolution, the sphere at μ = 4 −1.4 %. The ferrite path reproduces the dielectric numbers digit for digit, a check of both.
- **Approximations, stated in the game:** magnetized by the static magnets and coils only (levels with ramped or driven coils, outside fields or magnetic moments are refused: a changing magnetization would also induce an electric field, not modelled); no response to the particles' own magnetic fields (as the electrodes' image force); linear (no saturation, no hysteresis: soft ferrite below saturation).

## 2.8 Circuit parameters from the fields — *validated* (tests Z1–Z3, Z14; groundwork for circuits)

Lumped circuits will couple to the game's electrodes and coils through their capacitances, inductances and induced charges. These follow from the fields the engine already computes; the tests check them against Jackson before any circuit uses them.

- **Electrodes (Jackson §1.11–1.13).** The boundary-element solution gives the coefficients of capacitance through its unit systems (§2.7). Green's reciprocation gives the charge a point charge q at x induces on grounded electrode k: `Q_k = −q φ_k(x)`, with φ_k the potential when electrode k is at 1 and the others are grounded. A moving charge therefore drives the current `q v·E_k` into electrode k (the Shockley–Ramo theorem), which is how a passing particle will drive a circuit. At the verify resolution both hold within 4e-5 (Z1). At the preview resolution a charge closer to a plate than a panel (0.5 cells) induces 1 % off, so pickups will need the verify mesh near the beam.
- **Coils (Jackson §5.17).** The mutual inductance of two coils is the flux of one linked by the other, `M = (μ₀/4π) ∮ A₁·dl₂` per unit strength: Neumann's symmetric double integral, from the coils' exact vector potentials (`unit_vector_potential`). It holds to 1e-15 (Z2: Jackson Pr. 5.28's closed form, the Neumann integral by Wolfram, reciprocity between a circle and a square).
- **Self-inductance of a ring (Jackson Pr. 5.32).** A filament's own flux diverges, so a coil's self-inductance depends on its wire radius b. A current spread uniformly over the wire's surface links the ring's flux through the circles on that surface. The engine's vector potential gives `L = μ₀a[ln(8a/b) − 2 + (b/a)²((1/8) ln(8a/b) + 1/16) + O((b/a)⁴ ln)]` (Z3; the (b/a)² term was found and checked with Wolfram). That answers Jackson's question: the corrections are of order (b/a)², because the first-order term averages out around the wire. A uniform current inside the wire adds μ₀a/4, giving Jackson's `μ₀a[ln(8a/b) − 7/4]`.
- **Self-inductance of a polygonal coil.** The same construction as the ring's: the coil's own flux (its centre line's exact potential) through the closed curves on its wire's surface, the polygon offset in the plane by `b cos ψ` (corners mitred) at the height `b sin ψ`, averaged over ψ (`inductance::polygon_self`). Mutual inductances with circles and polygons are `∮ A·dl` along the polygon's sides (Gauss–Legendre). Z14: a 4 × 3 rectangle (wire 0.1) within 3e-16 of the same average by Wolfram at 30 digits; reciprocity to 7e-16; regular N-gons tend to `ring_self` with the polygon's geometric 1/N² (error ratio 3.8 per doubling). *Approximation:* the wire's surface current is taken uniform up to the corners, where a real current crowds to the inside: an error of order the wire radius over the side at each corner, not measured. A partial-inductance sum (Grover, Ruehli), tried first, carried an end error of +2b per corner (measured in the N-gon limit: ~1 % for the game's rectangles) and was replaced.
- **Not yet.** Resistance needs a conductivity, which no element has: the metal is perfect (§2.6–2.7). Jackson §8.1's surface impedance and §5.18's eddy currents come with the circuits design.

## 2.9 Circuits — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/circuit.rs`, tests Z4–Z9)

Lumped linear circuits, integrated by the stiff integrator RADAU5 (§5.4). The plan of the stage, and how circuits will drive the electrodes and coils, is in `docs/CIRCUITS.md`; this section is the engine.

- **Components.** Resistors, capacitors, inductors with mutual inductances, voltage sources (DC; AC `offset + V₀ sin(ωt + φ)`; trapezoidal pulses, optionally periodic), switches (a resistance while closed, open otherwise, toggled at set times), and capacitance blocks: a symmetric matrix over several nodes, such as the electrodes' Maxwell matrix (§2.7–2.8).
- **Units** as everywhere (k = 1, μ₀/4π = 1/c²): `Q = C V`, `V = R I`, `V = L dI/dt`. A capacitance is a length (a sphere of radius a has C = a), RC and L/R are times, and a coil's inductance is its geometric Neumann integral (§2.8) divided by c².
- **Lumped, a stated approximation.** Kirchhoff's laws: the circuit's own retardation and radiation are left out, valid while the circuit is small against `c/ω` at its frequencies.
- **Equations: modified nodal analysis.** Node 0 is ground; the unknowns are the other nodes' potentials, the inductors' currents and the sources' currents. Each node's current law, each inductor's `L dI/dt = v_a − v_b` (with the mutual inductances) and each source's `v_a − v_b = V(t)` make `M x' = A x + b(t)`: M holds the capacitances and inductances and is singular (nodes without capacitance, the sources' rows), A the conductances and the branches' incidence. Without loops of capacitors and voltage sources and cut sets of inductors this is a DAE of index 1, which RADAU5 integrates directly; otherwise the engine reports the circuit as singular (it is not physical without a resistance that the ideal components leave out, such as a switch opening on an inductor's current).
- **Breakpoints.** At switch toggles and pulse corners the integration restarts. A circuit of index 1 carries no impulsive currents, so the charges and fluxes `M x` are continuous there; the state after the breakpoint is the consistent one, found by Gaussian elimination: the rows of M keep `M x`, the remaining rows are the algebraic equations at the new time.
- **Integration.** RADAU5 with the exact constant Jacobian A and the mass matrix M, at relative tolerance `rtol` (absolute: `rtol` times the circuit's scale), the step limited by `h_max`. Its dense output, the collocation polynomial of each step, gives the potentials and the currents at any time.
- **The currents' rates** (a coil's induced field is `−κ̇ A_unit`) come from the inductors' law `L dI/dt = v_a − v_b` (with the mutual inductances) at the dense output's potentials, so they are as accurate as those. The derivative of the dense output itself is a power of the step less accurate (the first version used it: 1.9e-7 in Z8, against 1e-7 required). The two differ within a step by a cubic vanishing at the three collocation points, which integrates to zero over the step because Radau quadrature is exact to degree 4. So each step's change of a current is exactly the integral of its rate (unit test: 2.8e-16 over 348 steps). An inductor's current is therefore represented by that integral, from its value at the step's start (`CircuitSolution::current`, with the dense output's exact antiderivative, unit test `integral_of_the_dense_output`): its rate is exactly its derivative (a coil's induced field keeps Faraday's law with its field, §2.10), and it is more accurate than the dense output itself (Z8: 4.9e-12 against 2.9e-10).
- **Validated (Z4–Z9)** against closed forms: RC charging; an LC oscillation and its energy; charge sharing through a switch, whose heat `½ (C₁C₂/(C₁ + C₂)) V₀²` does not depend on the switch's resistance; a pulse through an RC (breakpoints); coupled inductors (mutual inductance); a driven lossy tank against its phasors, and the energy balance of source, resistors and stored energy. All within 1e-9 at tolerance 1e-12; each circuit takes milliseconds.

## 2.10 Driven electrodes and coils — *validated* (`crates/physics/src/drive.rs`, `inductance.rs`, tests Z10–Z13, Z15)

Circuits drive the electrodes' potentials and the coils' currents, circular and polygonal (the plan of the stage: `docs/CIRCUITS.md`).

- **Chains.** Each driven element ends a series chain: a source `V(t)` (§2.9) through a resistance into the element. An electrode's chain may have an inductance in series (an LC with its capacitance) or its resistance as a timed switch; a coil's chain may have a capacitance in series. The element closes the chain: an electrode through its capacitances, a coil through its inductance.
- **Capacitances.** The boundary-element Maxwell matrix (§2.7–2.8), as a block over the driven electrodes and, with them, the floating ones, whose charges the circuit then conserves; electrodes held at fixed potentials are grounds for the circuit.
- **Inductances.** A circular coil's self-inductance is that of a uniform current on its wire's surface, as on the game's perfect conductors (§2.8: `inductance::ring_self`); the driven coils' mutual inductances are their linked fluxes (`mutual_circles`). Both are the library functions that tests Z2 and Z3 check (to 1.9e-15), in units of `μ₀/4π = 1/c²`: `L = N/c²`, so a driven coil needs a finite c. A polygonal coil's self-inductance is its wire's surface flux (§2.8, `polygon_self`), its mutual inductances `∮ A·dl` along its sides (Z15: a driven square against the RL closed form within 2.3e-12, a square and a ring coupled through their mutual inductance within 3.4e-12 of the 2 × 2 closed form).
- **One-way coupling, a stated approximation.** The circuit is solved first (RADAU5 at tolerance 1e-12, §2.9, over all the time the flights can take), and the particles fly in the fields it sets. Left out is the particles' back-action: the charge they deposit on a plate, the currents they induce in its circuit (the Shockley–Ramo currents of §2.8) and the electromotive force they induce in a coil. The levels bound it (`docs/CIRCUITS.md`).
- **The fields.** An electrode's surface charge is the static solution's plus `(V_e(t) − V_e(0))` times its unit system's, for each electrode of the circuit (the solution is linear in the potentials). A driven coil has the field `κ(t) B_unit` and the induced field `−κ̇(t) A_unit` (quasi-static, §2.2), with `κ = I/c²`.
- **Exact induction.** The coil's current is the integral of its rate (§2.9: the inductors' law at the dense output's potentials) over each step of the circuit, so `κ̇` is exactly the derivative of `κ` and Faraday's law holds between the two fields. A first version took the current from the dense output itself, whose derivative differs from the rate by up to 1e-9 within a step.
- **Breakpoints and knots.** The flight's integration stops one ulp before each breakpoint (switch toggles, pulse corners) and restarts on it with the fields after it. Its steps also end on the circuit's step ends (knots), where the fields are continuous but their time derivatives are not; a step straddling knots integrates at reduced order.
  - Measured in Z11: without knots, the canonical angular momentum was conserved only to 7.9e-10, and it did not improve with the tolerance (1.1e-9 to 2.6e-10 at 1e-11 to 1e-14). The same flight in the exact closed-form fields, with the corners as breakpoints, conserves it to 1.6e-13. With knots: 3.3e-15.
  - The cost: the flight takes the circuit's steps where those are shorter than its own (Z11: 319 steps instead of 47).
- **Validated (Z10–Z13)** against closed forms: a plate charged through a resistor, with its capacitance from the boundary elements, and its field against the static solution at its potential; a coil driven by a pulse through a resistor, with the canonical angular momentum of a charge in it; a floating plate following a driven one through the capacitive divider; the impulse of a charging plate's field on a heavy charge, launched with a time offset.
- **In levels.** An optional `drive` on a level electrode or a circular coil (`level::Drive`: a source, DC, sine or pulse, a resistance, a series inductance or capacitance, a switch). A power supply on a tunable driven plate sets its source's level (a constant's value, a sine's amplitude, a pulse's top) instead of its potential, which stays the plate's potential at t = 0. The circuit is solved over every flight's lab time, up to the last launch plus `t_max`; the sandbox meters count its cost (`LevelField::setup_cost`). `Level::model_issues` rejects:
  - circuits with beams or free particles (the beam runner does not stop at breakpoints and knots);
  - driven coils at c = ∞ (no inductance), or with metal or magnetic moments (as ramped coils, §2.2);
  - floating driven plates, a series capacitance on a plate or an inductance on a coil, a switch with an inductance;
  - and circuits that cannot be solved (index above 1).
- **Neglected back-action, bounded.** `Level::drive_back_action_bound`: a charge q induces at most |q| on a plate, changing its potential by at most |q|/C against the largest potential the circuit's plates reach; a charge slower than c kept a wire radius b from a coil of radius a links at most the flux |q|·2πa/(c b), against the coil's self-flux N times the largest κ. The level test `drive_back_action_is_negligible` requires it below 1e-10 (as the image force); measured on the shipped circuit levels: 1.8e-12 to 3.2e-12 on the plates; on the coil of Induction kick 1.1e-16. The model notes show it, and the quasi-static parameter (the light time across the arena times the drives' fastest relative rate). The plate levels are Newtonian, where the quasi-static model is exact (0); the coil level needs a finite c and has c = 1e4, where it is 1.5e-4 (its corrections are of the square of the parameter, as the first-order retardation of a closed current vanishes).
- **In the game.** The worker solves the circuit with the preview field and sends its view: each driven potential and coil strength over lab time, plotted in the panel up to the end of the last flight with the launches marked, and the model notes (which need the solved circuit). The potential map and the field lines show driven plates and coils as they are at t = 0, without a dark region (the fields do work); the legend says so.


## 2.11 The z-invariant world — *in progress* (`crates/physics/src/zinv.rs`, `tube.rs`, `fdtd2d.rs`, tests V1–V19, F1–F7; the tubes, docs/TUBES.md)

For the vacuum tubes (the owner's decision, docs/TUBES.md): levels whose every source is translation-invariant along z. The plane is then a cross-section of the whole, and the fields are exactly those of line charges: real 3D electrostatics restricted by a symmetry, the one the textbook diode laws assume. A line charge λ (per unit length) has `φ = −2λ ln r`, `E = 2λ r̂/r` (k = 1).

- **Electrodes** are prisms along z; their cross-sections (rectangles, circles as polygons, and the frame of a tube's envelope: the outside of a rectangle, its inner face meshed) are cut into segments of constant surface density, collocated at the midpoints, with exact segment integrals (V1: against quadrature to 2.6e-15; on a segment the normal field's average, 0). Bias, unit systems and the capacitance matrix per unit length as in §2.7.
- **Reference.** A net line charge's potential grows like `−2Q ln r`: potentials are relative to the logarithm's zero (unit radius). Enclosed setups (a grounded or driven outer electrode, or zero net charge) do not depend on it; the validation uses only such, and every tube level is enclosed by its envelope (below).
- **Accuracy (V2, V3):** the coaxial capacitance per unit length `1/(2 ln(b/a))` and the image force on a line charge inside a grounded cylinder (the image −λ at R²/e) converge at second order: 2.1e-4 and 1.5e-4 off with segments of 0.05. The enclosure's net charge (0 in the continuum) is enforced only approximately by the collocation and shrinks at the same rate (V2; a first version of the test expected it exact).
- **Tubes** (`tube.rs`): macroparticles are lines of charge with the electrons' q/m (each follows an electron's trajectory exactly; only the space charge's graininess depends on the weight), Newtonian (v²/c² ~ 1e-3 at tube energies, neglected and stated). The force on each: the electrodes at their potentials, the surface charge every particle induces (re-solved each step from the stored factorization), and every other particle, each a uniformly charged rod of radius h (`SOFTENING`: an eighth of a segment in the levels, half a segment in the physics tests): its field is `2λ r⃗/h²` inside and exactly Coulomb's outside. *A compact softening (2026-10-13):* the former softening `2λ r⃗/(r² + ε²)` never quite became Coulomb's (ε²/r²: 0.4 % one cell away), so far groups of particles could not be summed as multipoles exactly (a tree code, for the many more particles a magnetized sheath needs: docs/TUBES.md). With the rods the calibration diode (below) converges more cleanly, V4's fine run is +6.68 % (was +7.79 %) and V15's at 0.25 of its bar; every tube level still verifies. The softening smooths the space charge's field over its length, and in a narrow gap the smoothed space charge limits the current too weakly: at half a segment the triode's output (its grid 1.25 from the cathode) came out 3.89, 3.38, 3.17 at refine 1, 2, 3 toward a limit of ~3.0, at an eighth 3.34, 3.12, 3.04 (halving the step, the weight or the mesh instead moved the preview by +0.03, +0.07 and −0.18 of its −0.50 to the verification); the game-scale diode, with a gap of 7, barely depends on it (1.113 to 1.105 at refine 2). Emission is space-charge limited by Gauss's law: each step each cathode segment emits the surface charge of the carriers' sign it holds, which drives the normal field there to zero. Particles are released at rest half a segment out (`EMISSION_OFFSET`): closer, a macroparticle's own image (an artifact of its finite weight) pulls it back. A particle that crosses an electrode's boundary is collected, and a carrier that comes back to its emitter is absorbed there on reaching its release layer (V12, below). Leapfrog with a fixed step.
- **Accuracy (V4):** the coaxial diode (radii 1 and 2, 1 V) against Langmuir–Blodgett, β² = 0.27926716071059 at b/a = 2 (`scripts/wolfram/v4_langmuir_blodgett.wls`, 30 digits; matches their 1923 table). The current is high by an error of first order in the resolution, from the skipped half-segment layer: +12.9 %, then +6.68 % with weight, step, segments and softening halved (observed order 0.94). The Richardson extrapolation is 0.50 % off (with the former softening and the quiet start, below: +15.3 %, +7.79 %, order 0.98, 0.24 %; before the quiet start +15.1 %, +7.83 %, 0.94, 0.6 %). (Before the far-field series and the reused induced charge below: +7.79 % and 0.5 %; the runs now take 1.2 s and 9.4 s instead of 3.2 s and 24.6 s.) Rejected: release at 1e-3 of a segment (−43 %, self-image) and release with the layer's analytic speed (+111 %, it emptied the space charge near the cathode).
- **Returning carriers (V12).** A macroparticle released at rest at δ (half a segment out) lacks the energy a real carrier gains over that first δ. In a static field it therefore comes back to δ with zero speed and turns again, never reaching the surface a real carrier would land on. In a triode with a negative grid the turned-back electrons bounced in front of the cathode for ever. A carrier that has been farther than 2δ from its emitter (`RETURN_LEAVE`) is therefore absorbed by it as soon as it is back within 1.5δ (`RETURN_ABSORB`). Not at δ itself: the carrier arrives there with zero speed, so whether it crossed would hinge on rounding. The rule's error is of the release's order, the last 0.5δ of the way back. V12 checks it with Little's law above Hull's cutoff. The coaxial magnetron of V8 at 1.1 B_H has a temperature-limited cathode of j = 1e-4 (its field 1e-4 of the electrodes'). Each electron then flies out to r_max and back, so the mean charge in flight divided by the emission rate is the time of flight. Its exact value comes from energy and canonical angular momentum, `v_r² = 2(φ(r) − φ(r₀)) − (B/2)²(r² − r₀²)²/r²`, integrated from the release radius to r_max and back to 1 + 1.5δ (substitutions remove the endpoint singularities). Measured: 3.500866 against 3.499402, 4.2e-4 off (required 5e-3: the step's quantization of release and absorption, dt/T ~ 1e-3). Without the rule the charge in flight grows without bound. V4, V6 and V9 do not change (nothing returns there). The magnetron with space charge below does.
- **Species, projectiles, magnetic field** (`tube.rs`). Several emitters (an electron cathode and an ion-emitting electrode: the bipolar diode), each with its own macroparticle weight (1/150 of its electrode's charge before emission); projectiles, single line charges with their own charge and mass per unit length, launched at given times, interacting with everything (their image included) and absorbed where they hit; a uniform B along z (a solenoid around the tube: z-invariant), with the Boris push (with B = 0 the kick-drift leapfrog, its two half kicks now added separately: the last bits of level 99's frames changed, golden hash updated). Electrode cross-sections: rectangles, solid circles, and rings (a tube's wall: open inside, both faces meshed, emitting from its inner face).
- **Bipolar diode (V6, `scripts/wolfram/v6_bipolar_coax.wls`):** the coaxial diode (radii 1 and 2, 1 V) whose anode, a ring, also emits ions (q/m = +1; the ions' mass enters only through I_i/√μ, so I_e does not depend on it). Reference: steady radial bipolar flow, shot from both emitters with the planar start and matched in the middle at 25 digits (its unipolar case reproduces Langmuir–Blodgett to 1e-13): I_e = 0.9647391802, I_i = 0.7785212006, the ions lifting the electron current 1.7146 times (the planar diode's 1.86 is the limit b/a → 1). Measured over t = 6–12: I_e 1.2910 then 1.1081 refined (+34 %, +15 %), I_i 1.0753, 0.9058 (+38 %, +16 %): first order, and the references lie at 0.52 and 0.50 of the verdict's error bar (1.5 × the runs' difference), as the game's verdicts assume. Bipolar flow carries larger errors than unipolar at the game's resolutions (both emitters skip their half-segment layer): goals with ions need wide ranges. The test takes 1.8 minutes and is run on demand (`--ignored`).
- **Projectile energy (V7):** a single line charge orbiting in a driven coaxial pair (no emitters) conserves `½mv² + λφ_static + ½λφ_induced` to 1.5e-5 of its scale over 2000 steps; its image energy is 3 % of the scale, so a missing or non-conservative image force would show 2000 times over.
- **Crossed fields (V8):** test electrons (charge 1e-9) launched at rest from the cathode of a coaxial magnetron (a = 1, b = 2, V = 1) against canonical angular momentum: below Hull's cutoff B_H = 1.88562 (at 0.9 B_H) all reach the anode; above (1.1 B_H) none, their largest radius 1.82050 for the exact 1.82071 at every launch angle (with the cathode's 0.1 polygon edges 1e-3 away it spread by ±0.007: launched 0.05 out on a 0.05 mesh). With space charge (an emitting cathode) the cloud above cutoff is unstable (the diocotron instability, seeded by the macroparticles) and carries current across the field. At 1.05 B_H it carries 6.7 % of the field-free current (0.0433 of 0.6477), while 0.080 returns to the cathode (the on-demand test `magnetron_with_space_charge_measurement`; measured, not validated: Hull's theorem covers steady axisymmetric flow only). The figure was 14 % before the return rule: the electrons that land back on the cathode had stayed in the cloud.
- **Temperature-limited emission (V9):** a cathode may have a saturation current density (Richardson's limit of its temperature): each step a segment emits the lesser of its surface charge (the space-charge limit) and the saturation over the step, so the current follows V^{3/2} up to the knee and then stays at the saturation whatever the field. V9: the coaxial diode with the saturation's total 0.126 far below the space-charge limit (0.56) collects j × perimeter to +1.1e-4 at V = 1 and −9.3e-5 at V = 4 (required 2 %: the macroparticles' quantization at the window's ends, at most 1.3 %). *Quiet start:* each segment's account of charge owed begins at a different fraction of a macroparticle (the golden-ratio sequence), so that segments emitting at the same rate (a temperature-limited cathode, a uniform field) do not emit together. Starting all at zero they did, in volleys: a coherent fluctuation of the current at the emission's cadence, V9's −0.9 % and +0.3 %, and on the screen a beam of regular arcs (the owner's report on the Klystron). With the quiet start V4's convergence improved (above) and the tube levels' verdicts moved within their bars.
- **Macroparticle weights** (`level/src/tube/build.rs`): 1/150 of the largest charge of its carriers' sign that the emitter's emitting face holds without particles (`Tube::emitting_charge`) over 16 phases of the sources' longest period (t = 0 alone without periodic sources), every circuit electrode at its source's value. In steady state the charge in flight is of that order (the planar diode's 4/3 of it), so this sets ~120–350 macroparticles in flight. Until the triode it was 1/150 of the whole electrode's charge at t = 0, which failed twice: an AC anode leaves the cathode without charge at t = 0 (the Rectifier's weight came from the floor alone), and non-emitting faces counted. Two guards. (1) A floor of 1/1000 of its charge with every other electrode at the level's largest voltage (`WEIGHT_FLOOR`): an emitter whose face holds no charge at any phase still emits what passing charges induce on it, and a weight from its own charge would be (almost) zero (a design scan once asked for ~1e300 macroparticles, a 25 GB allocation). At the former 1/20 the floor overrode a grid-screened cathode's own charge, which is far below what the level's largest voltage implies. A triode with B+ at 100 on a ±120 slider had 27 macroparticles in flight instead of 152, and its current came out 28 % low; with the new rule the slider's range no longer matters (118 in flight either way). (2) A temperature-limited cathode's weight is at most its saturation × perimeter / 50, so that it releases at least 50 macroparticles per unit time (`TL_RATE`): the Electron gun's beam had 36 in flight, sparse to the eye and grainy for the verdict. A run stops emitting at 20 000 macroparticles (`MAX_PARTICLES`) and its verdict is then uncertain, never a crash.
- **The emitter's mesh (V15).** The space-charge layer the macroparticles skip, released half of the emitter's segment out, is a first-order error in that distance (V4), and a narrow cathode–grid gap magnifies it: the triode's preview ran 29 % above its verification. The emitters are therefore meshed at a quarter of the other electrodes' segments (`EMITTER_MESH`). On V4's coaxial diode, the cathode at the others' size, a half and a quarter gave +15.1 %, +9.2 % and +5.4 % at V4's coarse resolution, and +7.8 %, +4.9 % and +1.0 % at its fine one. V15 requires the quarter to beat V4's uniform runs at both resolutions, and the fine run to lie within the verdict's bar (1.5 × the runs' difference) of Langmuir–Blodgett: it lies at 0.25 of it (0.19 with the former softening, 0.16 before the quiet start). In the levels the error bars shrank (Vacuum diode ±0.124 to ±0.059, Rectifier's mean ±0.72 to ±0.32, Sideways ±0.099 to ±0.022) and the values came down by the overestimate they carried (the diode at 26 from 1.203 to 1.113), at 30–60 % more run time. The triode's preview still ran ~15 % high after it: the particles' softening (see Tubes, above) was the rest, and with it at an eighth of a segment the preview is ~7 % high.
- **The player's solenoid:** a tube level may give the player a solenoid around the tube: a power supply on its terminal node sets the uniform B along z (from the limits' `solenoid_fields`, a slider like the other supplies). Sideways (level 100) uses it: above Hull's cutoff the electrons cannot reach the anode and drift along the cathode (E × B) into a collector beyond the gap's end. With the envelope the anode's current is 0 from B = 1.1. Its residue just below (0.13 of 0.96 at B = 0.95, against the planar cutoff B_H = 0.86) comes in at the gap's ends, where the translation symmetry behind Hull's theorem is broken. Above ~1.35 the electrons curl back onto the cathode, which now takes them (the return rule).
- **Projectiles of the player:** in tube levels the player's free charges are thrown in as projectiles (line charges with the level's free-charge mass), flying through the electrons, interacting with everything and absorbed where they land.
- **Ramo currents (V5):** a moving charge drives the current `dQ_i/dt` into each electrode, Q_i its induced charge (`charges_of` of `induced_by`), the input of the tubes' circuit coupling. A line charge between grounded coaxial cylinders against the exact `Q_a = −λ ln(b/r)/ln(b/a)` and `I_a = λ v/(r ln(b/a))`: second order, 6.5e-5 and 5.2e-5 off with segments of 0.025; the induced charges sum to −λ within 6.4e-7.
- **Circuits (V10, V11, V16–V19).** An electrode may be a circuit node, per unit length along z (consistent with the symmetry): a source (DC, sine or pulse) through a resistance R and a capacitance C to ground. R = 0 is an ideal source the electrode follows; R = ∞ is floating. Floating electrodes without a circuit are nodes too, of fixed charge. A node's charge is its conductor's (`Σ_j C_ij V_j` plus the particles' induced charge) plus its capacitor's. It changes by the resistor's current and by the charge of the particles the node absorbs or emits. Each step the node potentials are solved implicitly, by backward Euler in the resistors: `(C_NN + diag(C + dt/R)) V = Q + (dt/R) S(t + dt) − C_NF V_F − Q_ind`, then `Q += dt (S − V)/R`. The induced charge carries the Ramo currents (V5), so the particles and the circuit act on each other exactly. One ideal source may drive several electrodes (a grid's wires). *Straps.* Electrodes on any other circuit may be wired to its electrode (a magnetron's alternate vanes, strapped onto one tank): a branch with neither resistance nor coil to another electrode is a wire, and the electrodes it joins are one node at one potential, its charge balance summed over them (`C_NN` the sums of their blocks of the capacitance matrix, `Q_ind` of their induced charges). Without straps the arithmetic is the same as before (no golden hash changed). *Coils.* A branch may have an inductance L in series with R (a tank: a cavity's, biased by its own supply), and may return to another electrode instead of ground (a tank between two electrodes, as between a magnetron's vanes; the source's voltage then adds to that electrode's potential). An inductive branch's current I is an unknown of the same linear system, by the trapezoidal rule: it delivers `dt (I₀ + I₁)/2` over the step, with `L (I₁ − I₀) = dt/2 [(S + V_to − V − R I)₀ + (S + V_to − V − R I)₁]`. With the nodes' charge balance this is the implicit midpoint rule, which keeps a lossless LC tank's energy exactly and only detunes it, by `(ω dt)²/12` of its frequency; backward Euler would damp it as `e^{−ω² dt t/2}` (15 % of the amplitude over V17's six periods), a numerical loss that would set a resonator's Q. Resistive branches keep backward Euler, so the levels without coils did not change (their golden hashes are the same). In a level, every electrode on a source starts at the source's value at t = 0: the supply was on before the cathode heated, so its capacitors are charged. The player's supply on a tunable electrode sets the source's level (a DC value, a sine's amplitude, a pulse's high) or a sine's offset. V10: a node with R = 2 and C = 0.5 discharging, against the discrete sequence `(1 + dt/τ)^−n` to 3.4e-14, and the exponential within 0.499 of the bound `(t/τ)(dt/τ)`. V11: a temperature-limited beam (a constant current) charging a floating collector (C = 10 to ground) at `dV/dt = −I/(C₂₂ + C)`, measured to −2.9e-3 (required 2 %: V9's quantization of the current). V16, the energy balance the tubes' plan asked for: a line charge orbiting eccentrically in a coaxial pair (inner held at 1, outer a node through R = 2 to ground with C = 1) drives currents through R by the charge it induces, and its orbit is braked. With the field energy of conductors and a line charge `W = ½ Σ Q_i V_i + ½ λ φ_c(x)` (from the model's own surface charge), `½ m v² + W + ½ C V² + ∫ V²/R dt − ∫ V_in dQ_in` (the last the inner source's work) is conserved to 1.3e-3 of the Joule heat over t = 0–40 at dt = 0.002 and 3.2e-3 at 0.004 (required 2 %, and first order: the residual shrank 2.5×). With a coil (L = 5) in series with R the outer node is a damped LC tank (period about 20, Q ≈ 0.75) and the balance gains `½ L I²`: 7.2e-4 of the Joule heat at dt = 0.002 and 2.0e-3 at 0.004 (the same requirements; shrank 2.85×). V17: an LC tank, the inner cylinder of a coaxial pair at 1 with no current, through L = 1 to ground with C = 0.5 (`C = C₁₁ + 0.5 = 1.2206`, ω = 0.9052); the implicit midpoint rule maps `(√C V, √L I)` by a rotation through `θ = 2 atan(ω dt/2)` per step, and the run gives `V_n = cos(nθ)` to 6.1e-14 and keeps `½ C V² + ½ L I²` to 1.3e-13 over 4000 steps at dt = 0.01 (required 1e-11); its frequency is low by 6.827e-6, as `(ω dt)²/12` = 6.828e-6 predicts. V18: a tank between two electrodes, two wires (radius 0.5, 2.4 apart) in a grounded cylinder (radius 3), one at 1 and the other floating without charge, joined by L = 2: charge moves between them only, so `D = V_B − V_A` obeys `L C_eff D'' = −D` with `1/C_eff = eᵀ C⁻¹ e` from the wires' block of the capacitance matrix (`e = (1, −1)`); measured `D_n = D₀ cos(nθ)` to 6.3e-14 and `Q_A + Q_B` kept to 7.2e-16 (required 1e-11). V19: straps. Four wires (radius 0.5) at 1.5 from the centre of a grounded cylinder (radius 3), 90° apart: wires 0 and 2 strapped together and through L = 1 to ground, 1 and 3 grounded, 0 and 2 starting at 1. The pair is one node of `C = C₀₀ + C₀₂ + C₂₀ + C₂₂ = 0.7371`, so, as in V17, `V_n = cos(nθ)` with `ω = 1/√(LC)`: measured to 4.7e-14 and the energy kept to 1.0e-13 over 4000 steps (required 1e-11), the two wires' potentials equal to the last bit.
- **Tube levels** (`level/src/tube/`; the level format's `tube`): the electrodes (the level's, at the player's supplies, and the player's plates) are prisms; one is the cathode, optionally emitting only from its faces toward a given direction (its coating; within 60°). A level's electrode may be a box, a rod (a circle) or a ring (an annulus, open inside: a coaxial anode), the sections of V2–V4 and V6; the setup checks (touching, inside the board, starting in metal, the plates' clearance) use these outlines, a ring's hole being vacuum. Test `rods_and_rings` (`level/tests/tubes.rs`): through the level's own sections, a rod (radius 1) inside a ring (inner radius 2.5) has the coaxial capacitance per unit length `1/(2 ln 2.5)` = 0.545678 to −9.4e-4 with segments of 0.1 (required 1e-3, from V2's second-order convergence: 2.1e-4 at 0.05). The 3D levels refuse rods and rings (their boundary elements mesh boxes). Goals may be currents (charge per unit length per time, positive for arriving carriers) into electrodes, each averaged over a time window and counting one kind of carrier: the cathode's, or the ion source's (a bipolar level's ion current into the cathode, its wear; the electrode may not be the carriers' own emitter, which takes back only those that return), and goals on electrodes' potentials over windows: their mean, their swing (half the peak-to-peak), or the amplitude or the phase (relative to `sin ωt`, the sources' reference; compared modulo 360°) of their oscillation at a given ω, from the least-squares fit of `a + b t + c cos ωt + d sin ωt` over the window, all four together (exact for a sine on a line). *A correction:* the amplitude was first the Fourier amplitude after removing a fitted line, and the line took part of the sine with it (a sine is correlated with a line over any window), contrary to the claim that this was exact over whole periods: 1e-3 of the amplitude over 10 periods (found by the phase measure's test, `amplitude_and_phase`, which now requires the amplitude to 1e-9 and the phase to 1e-7°). The Triode's verified amplitude at its reference moved from 2.9337 to 2.9381 (its window is one period, but its output's phase keeps that error small there); its golden hash, which covers the runs and not the measures, is unchanged. A temperature-limited cathode's macroparticles per unit time (`particle_rate`, 50 by default) may be raised for a beam modulated at a high frequency (Klystron: 400, 1/400 of a period apart). Everything that breaks the symmetry (shots, charges, magnets, coils, spheres, dielectrics, ferrites, disturbances, driven electrodes, placed elements other than plates and supplies) is refused, as are touching electrodes, electrodes reaching beyond the board, and carriers faster than 0.1 c (the model is Newtonian; v²/c² = 1 % is below the macroparticles' error). Particles that leave the board end on the envelope and are counted as lost.
- **The envelope (V13).** Open sets of electrodes in the z-invariant world carry a net charge that depends on the logarithm's reference radius, and so did their far field. A positive electrode much larger than the unit radius had a negative net charge. The potential then kept rising away from it, pulling stray electrons off the board for ever. Shifting every potential of a triode by the same amount (physically meaningless in an enclosed tube) changed the electrons lost off the board from 0.20 to 0.36 to 0.54 per unit time, for shifts of −20, 0 and +20. A bigger board did not change it. Every tube level is therefore enclosed by a grounded envelope (`Section::Frame`), half a cell outside the board (`ENVELOPE_MARGIN`, so that a projectile launched from the edge starts inside). A real tube's envelope charges to about the cathode's potential, which is ground in every level. The envelope is meshed on its inner face only: inside a closed contour the potential is fixed by its values there (uniqueness), whatever lies outside. Its segments are 0.5 long at both resolutions (`ENVELOPE_SEGMENT`). V13 tests it with a line charge in a grounded 6 × 4 rectangle, against the exact Dirichlet Green's function (Gaussian units, `φ = Σ_n (8λ/n) sin(k_n x₀) sin(k_n x) sinh(k_n y<) sinh(k_n(b − y>))/sinh(k_n b)`, `k_n = nπ/a`, 2000 terms off the source's row). The error, at points 0.6–1 cell from the walls and of the potential's scale, is 3.6e-4, 3.4e-5 and 4.1e-6 with segments of 0.5, 0.25 and 0.125: order 3.08 (required ≥ 1.5, and 3e-3 at the envelope's 0.5). Segments of 1 gave 1.2e-2 and were rejected: a graded side's middle segments are π/2 times the nominal length. With the envelope the triode's losses vanished (−0.36 to −0.0006 per unit time), and the anode's capacitance became physical: it now faces a grounded conductor. The tube levels changed with it and were re-tuned (docs/LEVELS.md). The game-scale diode's current at 24 fell from 1.20 to 1.07. Sideways' collector peaks at B = 1.1 instead of 1.35. A plate below Through the cloud's gap now raises a potential hill (relative to the grounded edge) that its slow ion cannot climb.
- **Speed (V14).** The envelope tripled a level's segments and, with them, the run time, which goes as particles × segments. Three changes bought it back. (1) The push evaluates the segments' field alone (`segment_field`: half the logarithms and arctangents of `segment_integrals`, bit-identical). (2) Beyond 4 segment lengths (`FAR_SEGMENTS`) the push uses the field's multipole series instead (`segment_field_fast`). With ζ = x − midpoint and d = b − a as complex numbers, `E_x − i E_y = 2q/ζ + q d²/(6ζ³) + …`; the first omitted term is `(L/r)⁴/80` of the monopole. V14 checks 20 000 points from 4 to 100 lengths about segments of every orientation and length 0.05–2: worst 4.891e-5, at 4.00 lengths (the bound 4.88e-5; required 1e-4, far below the mesh's own error). (3) The particles' induced charge is solved once per step, at its end (the next step's emission and the display reuse it), plus the share of the particles added during the step. Before, it was solved three times per step. The game-scale diode's preview and verification now take 0.5 s and 2.5 s, and V4's runs are 2.6 times faster. With the emitters' finer mesh and weights from their emitting face, the tube levels' previews came to 0.7 s (Vacuum diode) to 1.1–2.3 s (Rectifier, Triode, Sideways, Electron gun, Through the cloud: its run was shortened from t = 60 to 40, at 60 its verification took 21 s), over the sandbox meter's 1 s guideline. (4) *Parallel loops and the inverse.* Timed per phase, a step's time was in the particles' fields (each particle against every segment and every other particle; 50 %) and the induced density (a logarithm per segment and particle, then the LU's two triangular solves; 45 %); emission, the circuit, the push and absorption together took under 10 %. The fields and the induced density's right-hand side are now computed in parallel (rayon), each particle's or segment's sum whole by one thread in its fixed order and collected in order: bit-identical to the serial loops, whatever the number of threads. The triangular solves, serial by nature, then took most of what was left; the induced density is now the collocation matrix's inverse (built once per setup, its columns solved in parallel from the LU) times the right-hand side, in parallel by rows, each row summed in order. That changes the rounding (the inverse's error is of the order of the LU's, cond · ε, far below the mesh's): every test of V1–V16 prints the same values to all their digits, but the tube levels' golden hashes changed, since a many-particle run amplifies a rounding difference into different emission events. Measured on the six levels' references, this rounding-level perturbation moved the verified results by up to 0.74 % (Triode's amplitude 2.9556 → 2.9337; Sideways −0.33 %, Through the cloud −0.29 %, Vacuum diode −0.21 %, Rectifier 7e-5, Electron gun unchanged; previews by at most 0.29 %): the macroparticles' noise, inside every verdict's bar (the closest, Through the cloud: a shift of 0.29 % against a bar of 0.82 %). Previews now take 0.24 s (Vacuum diode) to 0.48–0.63 s (the others), within the 1 s guideline, and verifications 0.9–2.6 s (were 4.4–13.9 s), on 16 hardware threads; V4's and V15's longest runs went from 9.4 s and 17.8 s to 1.6 s and 2.9 s. A tube's display streams from its first frame regardless.
- **The tree code** (`tree2d.rs`; test `tree_matches_direct_sums`). From `TREE_MIN` = 2000 particles in flight the step sums the particles' and the segments' fields at the particles, and the particles' potential at the segment midpoints (the induced charge), by quadtrees of complex multipoles: a group's potential outside its disc is `Re Ω`, `Ω = −2 [a₀ ln(z − z_c) − Σ aₙ/(n (z − z_c)ⁿ)]`, `aₙ = Σ q (z_k − z_c)ⁿ` (a segment's moments exact), `E_x − i E_y = 2 Σ aₙ/(z − z_c)^{n+1}`; a node is summed by its series (20 terms) when its radius is below half its distance and the target lies beyond its radius plus the rods' radius (so the softening never enters a series), every other source directly with its exact kernel. Required to 1e-7 of the field's and the potential's scale (four orders below the mesh's error and below `segment_field_fast`'s 4.9e-5); measured 1.2e-8 and 6.9e-11 on 2000 rods and 300 segments. Deterministic (a fixed construction and traversal, `libm`'s logarithm). Below `TREE_MIN` the direct sums are as fast and stay, so no shipped level's run changed (their golden hashes are the same). On the ion diode's scratch level (6000 particles) a preview takes 3.1 s instead of 6.9 s with the direct sums; at 3300, 1.7 s instead of 2.9 s. *What the particles bought:* with 8 times as many macroparticles the sheath's preview and verification differ by 8.3 % (6.64 and 6.09) instead of 16 % (8.21 and 6.88): the verdict's bar halves to ~12 %, the rest the mesh's and step's first-order error, which only a finer preview mesh would reduce further (~4 times the cost). *Speed (2026-10-13, the owner: the Ion diode "better optimized").* Profiled on the Ion diode's preview (600 steps, ~5900 particles at the end, 3.4 s): the trees' field evaluations 2.0 s, their two constructions and multipoles per step 0.6 s (serial), the induced charge 0.6 s.
