Bit-identical speed-ups: a node's radius from one square root of its line charges' largest squared distance (the root is monotonic and correctly rounded), the quadrants by a stable counting partition instead of a sort, the leaves' moments in parallel (each by one thread), one square root per visited node and a fixed traversal stack: the reference's result unchanged to the last digit (6.803114185688713), the preview 3.0 s. Then leaves of 24 sources instead of 32 (fewer direct pairs, more series; 16, 48 and 64 were slower): 2.7 s; the accuracy test 1.26e-8 (required 1e-7); results change within the sheath's own sensitivity to rounding (the reference's ion current 6.80–6.83 across leaf sizes). A fast multipole method (local expansions, M2L and L2L, with the same exact near field and the softening kept out of the series) was built and validated (3.3e-9 on 3000 rods with a dense clump and 300 segments) but was slower here: at 20 terms each translation costs 441 complex products, and its leaf-pair near zone held 2.5 times the direct pairs; with a wider opening angle (0.6) its error rose to 2.2e-7, over the requirement. Not kept.
- **Resolutions and verdict.** Preview: segments of 0.5 cells, 600 steps over the run, macroparticles of 1/150 of the emitting face's charge (above), the particles rods of an eighth of a segment, the emitters meshed at a quarter. Verification: all of these halved. The verdict takes the fine run's current with the error 1.5 × |fine − preview| (`ERROR_FACTOR`): met if that interval lies within the goal, missed if outside, uncertain otherwise. Calibrated on the game-scale planar diode (plates 10 long, faces 7 apart, V = 40, in its envelope; `level/tests/tubes.rs`): 2.19471, 2.14177, 2.11472 at refine 1, 2, 4. The differences shrink by a ratio of 0.51: first order, as in V4. The first-order limit is 2.0877, which the refine-2 run is off by 1.02 of its difference to refine 1; the error factor of 1.5 covers it. (Rods of a quarter segment, the same largest force as the former softening, converged more slowly: 2.18939, 2.14310, 2.10808, ratio 0.76, 1.51 of the difference, not covered; on Vacuum diode they gave a ratio of 0.51 and the eighth 0.34. With the former softening and the quiet start: 2.20803, 2.14575, 2.10675, ratio 0.63, the limit 2.0678 and 1.25; with the inverse, before the quiet start: 2.14708 and 2.11007, ratio 0.61, the limit 2.0731 and 1.21; before the inverse, which changed only the rounding: 2.14575 and 2.11339, ratio 0.52, 2.0810 and 1.04. The runs' noise (Speed, above) moves such ratios by this much.) (Softened at half a segment: 2.24266, 2.15637, 2.10941, ratio 0.54; before the emitter's finer mesh too: 2.48383, 2.32079, 2.24017; without the envelope the differences shrank 7.9×, which was the open boundary's doing.) The verdict takes the fine run with its wider bar rather than an extrapolation: designs with narrow gaps are not in the asymptotic range (the triode's preview ~15 % above its verification). The V^{3/2} scaling (exact for space-charge flow in any geometry) holds within the verdicts' errors: 8.015 for 8 between V = 10 and 40. Run times: preview 0.2 s, verification 1.0 s (were 0.7 s and 4.5 s before the parallel loops and the inverse; refine 4, 5.5 s).
- **Display.** The preview is streamed: run to the goal window's end, then the verification, then on to the level's `t_max` (Vacuum diode: 200), sent to the game in chunks of one time unit while it plays (1 unit per second at speed 1; computing is ~100 times faster). Every step is a frame with its particles and the electrodes' surface density, so the maps follow the animation. The game shows the particles over time, smoothly at any playback speed: between two stored steps each particle moves linearly (matched by its identity), and the electrodes' charges and potentials are interpolated (display only). By default the particles are drawn as the density of their charge, a glow on the map (a Gaussian 0.3 cells wide; electrons light blue, ions orange, each species full at its own 99th percentile of the charge per glow-sized cell, at least four overlapping macroparticles, and where both are present their colours weighted by their glows), so that a beam of many macroparticles reads as one stream, bright in its bunches, rather than as a snow of dots; or as dots (fainter the more there are), or not at all. With one scale for both, the Ion diode's dense, slow ions hid its fast electrons, so its leak could not be seen. Above 1500 particles the map (its shader sums every particle at every pixel, its arrows at every grid point) uses them merged per species in cells of a quarter cell (doubled until under 1500) at their charge's centroid: the map softens the space charge over half a cell anyway. On the Ion diode (~6000 particles) the game animates at ~116 frames per second instead of ~73. With it a potential map of the time shown (the electrodes' charge, applied and induced, and the particles softened over half a cell), in the carriers' energy relative to the cathode's potential at that time, in units of the largest potential difference to it the electrodes reach over the run (their own potentials and their sources' over a period, `TubeRun::potential_range`), with a gain control; or its electric field (|E| on a log scale over a chosen number of decades, 1.5 by default, below the board's 99th percentile, and arrows). Until the gain control the unit came from the potentials at t = 0, all 0 in a level with AC anodes: the scale collapsed, every colour saturated, and the map showed blue and red regions with a sharp wall between them where the potential is in fact smooth (the owner's report on the Rectifier). The panel plots each circuit electrode's potential over time, with the goals' windows marked. The magnetic (B_z) and energy-flow (Poynting) maps show the run's electromagnetic field (next paragraph). On boards above 1500 particles the map merges them (below); on tube levels the mouse wheel zooms about the pointer (Z: the whole board).
- **The triode** (level 106): five grid wires, 1.25 before a 6-long cathode, on one sine source (amplitude 1, period 40) whose offset, the bias, the player sets, and a 6-long anode through R = 60 to B+ = 100. Its gain is capped by the anode's own capacitance, which faces the grid and the grounded envelope: `g_m/(ωC)` came to ≈ 1.3 with a 14-long anode at period 20, hence the short anode and the slow signal. The output amplitude (verification, one period) rises from 1.96 at bias −1.2 through 2.76 at −0.8 and 3.49 at −0.4 to 4.09 at 0 (the preview ~7 % above, the bars ±0.3); it is cut off below about −2, where only the grid's capacitive feedthrough (0.32) is left. The goal 2.2–3.8 is verified for a bias of about −0.9 to −0.4: at 0 the gain is too high, at −1 too low.
- **The electromagnetic field (display; `fdtd2d.rs`, `level::tube_em`, `game/src/em.rs`; tests F1–F7, `em_field_keeps_gauss_law`, `closed_box_map_shows_the_circuit`, `em_field_obeys_amperes_law`, `magnetron_feed_field_is_smooth`, `hall_forces_balance`, `metal_drift_carries_the_current`).** The run's model is quasi-static: its carriers feel the instantaneous field. What its charges and currents radiate at a finite speed of light is computed beside it, one-way coupled (as the 3D levels' radiation views are), by Maxwell's equations for the transverse-electric field of the z-invariant world, Gaussian units: `∂B_z/∂t = −c (∂E_y/∂x − ∂E_x/∂y)`, `∂E/∂t = c ∇×(B_z ẑ) − 4πJ`, on a Yee grid (`E_x`, `E_y` on the cell edges, `B_z` at the centres, leapfrog, four grid cells per board cell, 0.9 of the stability limit `h/(c√2)`). The electrodes are perfect conductors (a staircase of grid nodes: the edges between metal nodes carry no field; charge arriving at a conductor's surface stays there as surface charge, redistributed by the field). Currents: every particle's move between frames (matched by identity), deposited by Esirkepov's charge-conserving scheme for linear shapes, once per frame interval and spread evenly over its steps (each step takes its share of the interval's current; until 2026-10-19 each step deposited its own part of every move, a step's cost growing with the particles: Klystron's field ran slower than its playback), so that a charge passes from its place at the interval's start to its place at its end in proportion to the time (a cell or less within an interval) and Gauss's law holds to rounding; a new particle comes out of its emitter's nearest grid node, an absorbed one goes into the electrode it reached; each electrode's circuit current flows along a straight lead (the owner's convention, 2026-10-12) from the innermost grounded ring enclosing it (an envelope), or in an open level from the nearest envelope outside it, or else from the board's border, the shortest such segment crossing no other electrode and no metal (a conductor; until 2026-10-19 the metals were not obstacles, and Positive carriers' inner Hall contacts were wired through the other strip; test `leads_cross_no_metal`, on every shipped level with metals), carrying the electrode's change of charge (from the frames' surface densities) less the particles it exchanged plus what its own children's leads drew; every lead's ends are snapped to nodes of their metal (a point near a thin vane would spread part of its charge onto vacuum nodes, a spurious charge left behind). So each conductor's charge follows the run's. The start is the electrostatic field of the first frame's potentials and charges solved on the same grid (conjugate gradients), a stationary solution of the scheme. A level with an output (the magnetron) is open: a convolutional PML (Roden and Gedney, 16 cells, cubic grading, design reflection 1e-8) inside the board's edge absorbs the outgoing waves, and its outermost envelope has no lead (a z-invariant world without currents from infinity keeps its total charge); otherwise the border is a grounded box (the tube's envelope). The display's speed of light is the output's (the magnetron: c = 40, the electrons at most ~0.24 c) or, in a closed box, ten times the carriers' largest speed (what the potentials give them, a metal's `4 √(kT/m)`, a projectile's launch speed) and at least `12 ω_f L/π`, so that the box's lowest mode `ω₁ = πc/L` (L its longer side) lies twelve times above the run's fastest frequency `ω_f` (its sources' ω, a pulse's `1/rise`, the carriers' cyclotron frequency `|q/m| B` in the run's field): the box then follows the run quasi-statically instead of ringing as a resonator (Klystron's buncher, ω = 2π, lay 9 % below its box's lowest mode at the old c = 114, and the map showed the mode's standing bands across the board; now c = 1200). *A closed box's smoothing.* A lossless box keeps every wave radiated in it, and the run's macroparticles radiate far more than its real carriers would: each macroparticle stopping on an electrode (transition radiation, its weight times a real electron's in power), their graininess, and their paths' kinks at the frames. Measured on Triode (the field's `B_z` sampled every 0.02 over 4 time units, the energies of its mean and of the fluctuation about it): the fluctuation was 0.44 of the mean's energy at t = 4, 4.8 times it at t = 12 and 140 times at t = 36 (0.060 against 4.3e-4), still growing, so the map showed random blotches; without the absorbed particles' last moves it was 0.0073 at t = 36, without the births 0.060 and without the leads 0.057. The maps therefore show a closed box's field smoothed in time: four first-order low-passes in cascade (`y += α(x − y)` each step, `α = 1 − e^{−Δt/τ}`, `τ = 2/ω₁`), which pass a static field unchanged and a frequency ω as `(1 + (ωτ)²)^{−2}` (the box's lowest mode by 0.04, its higher modes far more; the run's fastest frequency by at least 0.95). Being linear, the scheme's smoothed field is exactly the field of the run's currents smoothed by the same kernel (a gamma distribution); its mean delay `4Δt(1 − α)/α ≈ 4τ = 8L/(πc)` (Vacuum diode: 0.91) is taken out by advancing the field that much further than the time shown. Measured the same way: Triode's fluctuation at t = 36 fell from 0.060 to 8.4e-6, against the mean's 1.2e-4 (what remains includes its grid's sine); Vacuum diode 1.1e-5 against 6.7e-2, Sideways 1.6e-5 against 5.9e-2, Hall probe 2.2e-3 against 0.31 (its carriers' thermal currents). Every level's field, open ones too, is also smoothed over the run's sampling, each stage's time constant at least 0.4 frame intervals (`EM_FRAME_RATIO`): the run's currents are known at its frames only and step from one to the next, and the steps radiate at the frames' rate, above their Nyquist frequency π/T where the run has no information (Magnetron, frames every 0.05: stripes along its antenna's feed alternating every 1.5–2, ω ≈ 126, and inside its closed anode block square-edged fragments, both strongest where the real field is weak). The cascade passes `2π/T` as 0.02 and Magnetron's ω = 3 and 6 as 0.993 and 0.97; its radiated power, measured on the field itself, is unchanged. A closed box takes the longer of the two time constants (the frames' sets it only for the metals' levels, whose frames are sparse: Hall probe's every 0.35). Cost: a field step of the 200 × 88 Klystron box took ~0.14 ms at first (0.04 ms the solver), a second per time unit at its c = 1200, slower than its playback; with each interval's current deposited once, the smoothing's stages in one parallel pass and `E_x`, `E_y` updated together, ~0.05 ms (0.36 s per time unit while the game runs beside it); the outflow contour, ~0.01 ms a step, is measured in open grids only. The game's field thread has a thread pool of its own (half the cores: in the shared pool its small parallel steps waited behind the preview's and verification's tasks) and sends the field it has reached every 16 ms of wall time (slices of 0.5 time units had made a lagging field, and the playback held back with it, jump by half Klystron's period). The game advances the field on a thread of its own with the animation (playback waits for it). The maps: B_z (orange out of the plane, teal into it) and |S| = (c/4π)|E × B_z ẑ| with arrows, each on a log scale below the run's largest 99th percentile so far, for a tube that radiates (an output) taken over the board outside its rings (over the whole board the tube's inside, a few per cent of it with the strongest fields, set the unit and the radiation lay decades below, black); the panel shows the power leaving through a contour inside the absorbing layer, averaged over two time units, beside the load's R⟨I²⟩. The field follows the playback once shown, also under the other maps; playback waits for it by holding the time shown, never setting it back (an earlier time restarts the field from t = 0: a field left behind under another map once pulled the playback back to it, stuck near the start). The electric map's arrows and colour unit sum the segments' far fields by their multipoles (`TubeRun::e_at`, within 5e-5) on at most about 32 × 32 points: on the Magnetron's 96 × 96 board its every second node, twice a frame, had made it a slideshow (about 3 frames per second; 55 now). Validated: F1, a square cavity's TE₁₀ mode oscillates at the scheme's exact discrete frequency to 5.8e-9 (required 1e-4; the continuum's lies 1.5e-4 away, the scheme's dispersion); F2, Gauss's law with ten charges crossing cells beside a charged conductor for 300 steps, to 5.1e-15 of the peak density (required 1e-10); F3, a charged conductor's static field is stationary to 1e-15 over 500 steps (required 1e-12); F4, an oscillating current element's `B_z` against the steady cylindrical wave `Re[(iπ K̃ k/c) H₁⁽¹⁾(kr) sin φ e^{−iωt}]` at 1.5 and 2.5 wavelengths: amplitude +0.14 %, phase 0.3°–0.5° (required 3 %, 3°); F5, the absorbing layer's reflection 1.2e-4 of a pulse's peak (required 1e-3); F6, the element's radiated power through a square, `outflow`, against `π ω K₀²/(4c²)`: +0.72 % (required 3 %); F7 (`level::tube_em`), a closed box's smoothing on F1's ringing mode over a uniform `B_z` (a static solution), at `ωτ = 2`: after 70 time constants the shown mode is the field's times the cascade's exact discrete transfer `H(ω)⁴`, `H = α/(1 − (1 − α)e^{−iωΔt})`, to 2.7e-14 in complex amplitude (fitted over 10 periods; required 1e-6), `|H|⁴` = 0.0400167 (the continuum's 0.04), and the static part unchanged to 7e-16 (required 1e-9); `closed_box_map_shows_the_circuit`, Vacuum diode's reference preview over its steady current (t = 12–20, every 0.05): the shown `B_z`'s fluctuation 1.3e-4 of its mean's energy (required 1 %: 10 % in amplitude, below what the map's logarithmic colours show), the field's own 0.36 (0.46 depositing each step's part of the moves, whose kinks between frames radiated); `magnetron_feed_field_is_smooth`: across the antenna's feed (x = 62, y = 44–52 every 0.2, the feed's line left out) over t = 109–110, where the feed's current passes zero, the shown `B_z`'s largest second difference is 0.05 % of the largest field beside the feed (required 1 %, about what the map's logarithmic colours show; 4.5 % unsmoothed); `em_field_obeys_amperes_law`, the magnetic field's size: across a lead carrying a current I the shown `B_z` must jump by `4πI/c` (Ampère), I the lead's own current from the run's charges (independent of the field), the jump between the means over squares of side 1 one unit either side of the lead's midpoint, over windows where the displacement current through that path averages out (Vacuum diode's beam over t = 12–20 and Hall probe's metal over 20–40, both steady; Triode over 40–80, a whole period of its grid's signal); every lead carrying a fifth of its level's largest current with no other lead within 2: +0.13 % and +0.05 % (the diode's cathode and anode), +0.13 % and +0.25 % (the Hall probe's ends), −1.04 % and −0.11 % (the triode's cathode and anode; required 3 %). Over 12–20, half a period, the triode's read +7.7 % and +0.9 %: the shown field is centred on its time, the lead currents were taken half a time unit later (the smoothing's delay) while its current swings. Where the currents change, as in the Triode (its grid's signal, its anode swinging through its load), the displacement currents through the vacuum and the grid's own leads make the field outside a circuit's loop uneven (Triode at t = 15: +0.0054 at its lower left, +0.0014 at its upper right, which the map's logarithmic colours draw dark); the magnetic and energy-flow maps draw the leads (owner's decision of 2026-10-12) with beads that move with the charge each has carried as the shown field sees it (the lead currents smoothed by the field's own stages), and every electrode whose potential swings over the run glows with its departure from its mean (warm above, cool below): Magnetron's tank vanes and the antenna strapped to them pulse together, its antenna's feed sloshing in step with the tank's leads inside the block; on Bipolar diode's preview run (electrons and ions emitted and absorbed, the circuits' leads), Gauss's law at every vacuum node to 5.3e-10 of the peak density over 73 checks (required 1e-9; 5.7e-10 depositing each step's part of the moves). A fast multipole method was tried for the particles' sums before this (above); the field solver is separate.
- **The ion diode** (level 104): an inverted coaxial diode, the ring the cathode and the rod the anode emitting ions (q/m = 1/16), in the player's solenoid: above the electrons' Hull cutoff they stay in a sheath along the ring, whose charge draws more ions across (the magnetically insulated ion diode); the ions' own cutoff is 18 times higher. The sheath's current is the most resolution-hungry quantity of the stage: with the usual macroparticles it depended mostly on their number (×1 to ×8: 8.21, 7.42, 6.90, 6.66 at 28 V, B = 1.8; verdict bars of 15–25 %). A level may therefore ask for more: `particles` (every emitter's macroparticles times it; 8 here, converged in their number: ×16 gave 8.30 against 8.19 at 30 V, B = 1.7, at the tree code's cost) and `emitter_mesh` (the emitters' segments that many times finer; 2 here: the release layer's first-order error, which the sheath magnifies, brought the preview from 9.8 % to 7.3 % above the verification at 30 V, B = 1.7 (4.8 % at B = 2.4); 4 times finer, 4.9 %, but a verification of 29 s). A grounded envelope (a thin ring around the cathode) closes the tube: in the open 2D world the cathode ring's outer surface carried a charge set by the logarithm's reference, whose field drew electrons off its outer face into an arc drifting round it (E × B) off the board, a glitch in the owner's review. The leak the level describes is within both sliders: at 30 V with the field off 17.4 reaches the rod, and at B = 1.9 above ~50 V (0.066 at 50, 0.25 at 60, 3.6 at 80, the supply's range now ±80). Verified at the reference (30 V, B = 1.9): ions 6.43 ± 0.55, no leak; preview 2.9 s (over the 1 s guideline: the stage's costliest level), verification 12.4 s.
- **The magnetron** (level 108): a strapped eight-vane magnetron. The cathode is a rod (radius a = 2) on the player's supply; the vanes' tips are at b = 4.5 and the anode block is a ring. The vanes at 0°, 90°, 180°, 270° and the block are grounded, and the diagonal ones are strapped (one node) onto a tank to ground: L = 0.036 in series with R = 0.002, plus 1.34 to ground. With the vanes' own 1.337 (fitted from the tank's ringing driven through its coil with no emission) the cold resonance is 3.22, Q ≈ 55. Its oscillation is the π mode, four crests around the vanes turning at ω/4. In the player's solenoid field the electrons form a hub around the cathode, and where their drift keeps step with the wave they bunch into four spokes that feed it. The oscillating band follows the Buneman–Hartree condition `V = Ω B (b′² − a²)/2 − Ω² b′²/2` with `Ω = ω/4 = 0.77` and an effective anode radius b′ ≈ 5, between the vanes' tips and roots: the band's lower edges are 40, 42.5 and 47.5 at B = 5.5, 6 and 6.5, against 37, 41 and 45 from the formula. Its upper edge lies far below Hull's cutoff: there the electrons outrun the wave. The design had three findings. (i) Without the added capacitance the electrons pulled the frequency between two branches, near 3.0 and 3.6, and the oscillating cells were not contiguous; the capacitance halves the pull. (ii) The start-up comes from the switch-on transient (the cathode's step rings the tank to about 4) and from the particles' noise, so it depends on the resolution: from t ≈ 20 to t ≈ 80 at 1 to 4 times the macroparticles. An injected priming signal did not make it reliable, because below threshold the hub absorbs it. The saturated swing does converge (45 V, B = 6: preview 14.6, verification 15.6, 4 times the macroparticles 16.0), so the goal's window is late, t = 90–120, after every resolution has started. (iii) A 4-vane anode (n = 2) did not oscillate at all. Verified at the reference (45 V, B = 6): swing 15.66 ± 1.20; preview 1.1 s, verification 5.3 s. *Its output.* A magnetron exists to radiate (the owner: "I necessarily need to see this emission", and then: on the map, from all the charges). A closed grounded anode shields its interior, so the waves must leave through an output: a rod (diameter 2) 20 cells from the axis, outside the block, strapped to the tank's vanes (`tube.output`: the tank's load and c = 40), on a board of 96 × 96 cells (about a wavelength, λ = 2πc/ω ≈ 82, across). Its potential follows the vanes', its charge oscillates, and the current feeding it flows along its lead from the block: the field solver (above) shows the waves leaving it in the magnetic and energy-flow maps. The antenna's radiation resistance, measured by the solver, is the tank's load: with R = 0.002 the power leaving the board was 0.91 of the load's R⟨I²⟩ (19–21 against 20–23 from t = 90 on), so R = 0.0018: then 20.8 against 21.0, 19.7 against 19.1, 20.3 against 20.3, 20.3 against 19.6 and 19.7 against 19.0 at t = 90–130, the energy the circuit's load takes is what the Maxwell field carries away to within ~3 %. The absorbing layer then lay on the board's outer cells, where it visibly damped the waves (the owner: the fronts seemed to stick to the edge); it now lies outside the board behind one cell of free space (`EM_MARGIN`): over t = 98–100 the power out is 20.0 against the load's 19.1 (+4.6 %; test `magnetron_antenna_radiates_its_load`, required 10 %). Verified at the reference (45 V, B = 6): swing 16.80 ± 0.43; preview 2.1 s (the larger board's envelope has more segments), verification 8.1 s; the field took ~0.07 s per time unit on the board alone (392 × 392 cells), faster than playback; with the margin and the layer outside it the grid is 432 × 432 (21 % more). The playback loops once the field has reached the run's end (the wait for the field had held it there). The player may average a tube's maps over time, as a meter integrates (`game/src/average.rs`; display only): a first-order low-pass `x̄ ← x̄ + (x − x̄)(1 − e^{−Δt/τ})` over the run's time, of the sources for the potential and electric maps (the charge binned on the map's cells and the electrodes' surface densities: the maps are linear in them) and of B_z and S per sample for the electromagnetic maps (the mean S is what a power meter reads: the radiation pattern); τ = 0, the default, shows the instantaneous field.
- **The klystron** (level 107; test `klystron_against_bunching_theory`, `level/tests/tubes.rs`): a weak beam from a Wehnelt gun (the cathode at −V₀ between two plates at its potential; the drift tube grounded) passes a buncher (two plates on a sine of amplitude V₁, period 1: its entrance and exit gaps modulate the speeds) and, 21 further, a catcher plate in a tank (L = 0.051, R = 0.04 to ground, resonant at ω = 2π with the plate's own 0.497). Its space charge is negligible, so the bunching is ballistic and small-signal theory applies (Webster): from the level's own fields, the buncher at 1 V gives an electron crossing it at v₀ the energy `V₁ Re(G e^{iωt₀})`, `G = ∫ E_x e^{iωx/v₀} dx` along the axis, so `Δv/v₀ = V₁|G|/(2V₀)`, and after the drift L the beam current's fundamental is `2 I₀ J₁(X)`, `X = (Δv/v₀) ω L/v₀`. At V₀ = 50: |G| = 0.627, θ₀ = ωL/v₀ = 13.20, so the predicted optimum drive (X = 1.841) is V₁ = 22.26; the preview's catcher amplitude peaks at 19.19 (−13.8 %; required 15 %, fixed beforehand: at the optimum the speeds are modulated by ~14 %, where the small-signal corrections are of that order). The rising side follows J₁: at half the measured optimum's drive the amplitude is 0.703 of its largest, J₁(0.92)/J₁(1.841) = 0.710 (required 0.05). A first version of the test compared the shape at fractions of the predicted optimum, which counts the optimum's allowed 15 % twice, and failed by 0.09. Past the optimum the thin-gap theory does not hold here: at 1.5 times the optimum's drive the amplitude is 0.824 of its largest against the thin-gap 0.726 (a buncher of two gaps 7 apart, between which the electrons already drift, and a catcher 4 long, whose coupling weighs fast and slow electrons differently: measured, not required). The output's phase follows the drift angle: 7.5° per volt near V₀ = 50 for θ₀/(2V₀) = 7.6 (phase pushing). Refine 1 to 2: phases within 2.6°, amplitudes 5 %. Preview 0.64 s, verification 3.5 s.
- **Next** (docs/TUBES.md): a bipolar level; an in-plane circuit path, for magnetic maps.

### Conductors: the Drude metal — *in progress* (`crates/physics/src/lattice.rs`, `tube.rs`; tests D1–D11 in `crates/physics/tests/drude.rs`; plan and decisions in docs/DRUDE.md)

A conductor's interior, stated as a model: classical carriers scattering off fixed ion cores (a real metal's conduction electrons are a degenerate quantum gas; this is a scaled classical plasma whose screening length and mean free path are well below the conductor's size, so that Ohm's law, Joule heating and the Hall effect emerge as they do in a metal).

- **The conductor** is an axis-aligned rectangle of the z-invariant world holding carriers (the tubes' macroparticles, line charges) over a uniform background of the opposite charge (the ions', jellium). The background's potential and field are in closed form (`zinv::rect_integrals`: with `F(u, v) = uv ln(u² + v²) − 3uv + u² atan(v/u) + v² atan(u/v)` summed over the corners; test `rect_field_gauss_and_far_field`), enter the push and the electrodes' induced charge, and the conductor is neutral at the start. Its walls reflect the carriers specularly, except where an electrode lies beyond (a contact), which absorbs them. **Ohmic contacts** (`Emitter::ohmic`, `OhmicContact`; the boundary condition of device Monte Carlo codes): each step, each face segment of a contact counts the carriers in the layer in front of it (half a mean free path deep, at most a quarter of the metal's width: `Level::contact_depth`) and gives off, at the carriers' weight, what that layer lacks to be neutral, from its face as a gas at the lattice's temperature crosses a wall (`lattice::flux_velocity`: the flux-weighted law, 1.5 kT each in the plane, what the carriers reaching the face bring). Three earlier rules, measured: (0) the same count with the carriers placed at random in the layer with a Maxwellian's velocities (kT each): every exchange through a contact took 0.5 kT from the metal, a heat sink in proportion to the contact's length; a wide strip's lattice heat read −32 per time where its `IV` was +13, and Ohm's law carried 2.85 at 30 (now 3.00); (1) the cathodes' space-charge limit with carriers released at rest: a contact was a cold reservoir (it absorbs the thermal flux that reaches it, ~25 charge units per time in a level's metal, and the lattice reheated what it gave back: the Joule heat read −580 per time where `IV` is +126, the carriers next to the contacts ran at 0.6 of the temperature, the verified current's error bar was ±7 %); (2) the space-charge limit with the carriers released as a gas at the lattice's temperature crosses a wall (the normal speed Rayleigh-distributed, `lattice::flux_velocity`): the heat right, the error bar ±2.4 %, but the field held at zero on an injecting contact's face piled carriers in front of it, a layer with its own voltage: along a strip 18 long at 30 the potential was flat for 1.5 next to the injecting end, a third of the way along it read 8 instead of 10 (the potentiometer's side contact drew no current at 8.65; 7.8 with the carriers twice as hot). With ohmic contacts the self-consistent resistor's bulk field is V/L to 0.2 %; the Potentiometer's side contact draws no current at 9.05 for 30 (9.5 with rule (0)): the contacts take about a volt. The carriers start Maxwellian on a jittered grid (a quiet start).
- **The ion cores** are hard discs of radius `a`, `n_ion` per area, placed independently at random (an amorphous metal; the Boolean model, whose free paths are exactly exponential with the rate `κ v`, `κ = 2 a n_ion`, the mean free path `ℓ = 1/(2 a n_ion)`). A first version jittered a square lattice by less than a cell, to keep the cores apart: its rows left open corridors 0.04 wide, free paths without end (an infinite-horizon Lorentz gas): the conductivity depended on the setup (22 % below the theory between contacts, 10 % in a periodic box) and settled slowly. Even a lattice jittered over whole cells is more regular than random (free paths counted in cells, geometric: about −14 % at `2a/s = 0.28`).
- **A collision** is a specular bounce off the core with a new speed drawn from `g(v) = √(2/π) v² e^{−v²/2σ²}/σ³` (`σ² = kT/m`): the collision rate grows as v, so this is the speed law of the carriers that collide in a Maxwellian gas, which then stays Maxwellian at rest (it is the 3D Maxwell law). The lattice takes the energy (a bath at a fixed temperature): the Joule heat, `TubeState.joule`. Random numbers come from a hash of (seed, carrier, step, collision): reproducible whatever the order the carriers are handled in.
- **The step** (`Lattice::advance`): the push (Boris, as in the tubes) gives the stored half-step velocity `v₁` from `v₀`; the kick `g = v₁ − v₀` is spread over the step (the velocity at the fraction φ of it is `v₁ + g(φ − ½)`, the path a parabola). A collision is found on the path's chord (the cores bucketed by cells), the core's normal taken there, and the carrier placed on the parabola at that moment; then the rest of the step is followed the same way, up to four collisions. The field's work up to a collision then equals the kinetic energy gained, and energy balances to rounding (D3). Two earlier versions: the whole kick before the collision and the rest of the step unchecked (the collision rate 2.7 % low at dt = 0.02, by the missed second collisions); the normal taken at the parabola's point, which lies `g dt/8` against the field off the surface, so the bounce leaned with the field (σ 11 % low at `a = 0.00125`, growing as the cores shrink).
- **Measuring a current**: the charge carried, from the carriers' moves. The average of the stored velocity is not the current: after a collision it holds the new velocity for the whole step, and the drift reads low by about `κ⟨v⟩ dt (1 − ⟨cos θ⟩)/2` (8 % at dt = 0.02). This artefact, not the physics, made the first measurements look dt-dependent.

**The Boltzmann equation of the model** (linear response; the references of the tests). With `f = f₀ + f₁`, `f₁ = F(v) cos φ + G(v) sin φ` (velocity angle φ), the collision operator is `−κ v f₁ + (g(v)/v) ⟨cos θ⟩ A` with `A = ∫ κ v F v dv`: a specular bounce at a uniform impact parameter turns the velocity by θ with `cos θ = −1 + 2β²` (β uniform on [−1, 1]), so `⟨cos θ⟩ = −1/3`. The speed law g drops out:

$\sigma = \frac{n q^2}{m}\,\frac{\sqrt{\pi/2}}{2\,\sigma_v\,\kappa\,(1-\langle\cos\theta\rangle)} = \frac{3}{16}\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\,\frac{\rho\,|q/m|}{a\,n_{\rm ion}\,\sigma_v}$

(`ρ = nq` the carriers' charge density). In a magnetic field B_z (`ω_c = qB/m`), with `Z = F + iG` the same steps give the complex conductivity `σ̃ = (j_x + i j_y)/(E_x + i E_y)`:

$\tilde\sigma = \frac{\pi q^2}{m \sigma_v^2 \kappa}\,\frac{P}{1-\langle\cos\theta\rangle Q},\quad P = \kappa\!\int_0^\infty\!\frac{v^3 f_0}{\kappa v + i\omega_c}\,dv,\quad Q = \kappa\!\int_0^\infty\!\frac{v\,g}{\kappa v + i\omega_c}\,dv$

and the Hall coefficient `R_H = Im(1/σ̃)/B`; in a weak field `R_H = (4/π)/(nq)`: the Hall factor 4/π of a collision rate ∝ v (as for a constant mean free path; neither the bounce's memory nor the speed law enters). Diffusion: `D = μ kT/m` (Einstein), μ the mobility. A Monte Carlo of this collision law without geometry (collisions drawn at the rate κv) reproduces σ to 1 % (+1.1 ± 0.9 % at a field giving `qEℓ/kT = 0.05`).

**Validation** (`cargo test --release -p physics --test drude -- --nocapture --test-threads 1`). Carriers in a box 8 × 8 periodic both ways over random cores (`ℓ = 0.5`, `a = 0.00125`: `a² n_ion = 0.00125`), 200 000 carriers of thermal speed 1, dt = 0.02, the window t = 5–40; an acceleration 0.2 along x where a field is applied (`qEℓ/kT = 0.1`).

| Test | Quantity | Reference | Required | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | mobility (drift from the moves, per acceleration) | `(3/8)√(π/2)/κ` | 5 % | −1.83 % |
| D1 | collision rate per carrier | `κ σ_v √(π/2)` | 1 % | +0.27 % |
| D2 | drift under `0.2 cos ωt`, ω = 2.5 (`ωτ ≈ 1`), in phase and a quarter period behind, over 14 periods | the Boltzmann equation's at that frequency (`κv − iω` for `κv`: `mobility` at −ω) | 5 % of its size | 2.3 % |
| D3 | field's work − heat − kinetic energy change | 0 | 1e-9 of the work | −1.8e-12 |
| D4 | drift at `ω_c = 1.25` (`ω_c τ ≈ 0.5`), both components | `σ̃` above (quadrature) | 5 % of its size | 1.16 % |
| D4 | weak-field Hall factor of the quadrature | 4/π | 1e-4 | 1.27324 |
| D5 (a metal in `Tube`, between two plates at ±0.5, no contacts; 32 000 carriers, t = 5–30) | the decay length of the carriers' departure from the background at both walls (log fit, bands weighted by inverse variance) | the Debye length `σ_v/√(4πρ|q/m|)` = 0.282 | 10 % | +1.0 % |
| D6 | diffusion, the slope of `⟨Δx²⟩/2` over t = 25–45 | `μ kT/m` | 5 % | −0.36 % |
| D7 (resistor 16 × 4 as below at 1.6 V, 2000 carriers, 16 seeds over t = 8–1608; `--ignored`, 16 min) | the charge carried, `Q(t)` (the carriers' moves along the length): its spread about the mean drift, `⟨(ΔQ − ⟨I⟩T)²⟩`, its slope between T = 1 and 4 (the current's correlations are gone by T ≈ 0.5) | `2kT/R` (Nyquist: fluctuation and dissipation), R = V/⟨I⟩ from the same runs, kT a macroparticle's `\|q_c\| σ_v²/\|q/m\|` | 5 % | 0.0 % (statistics 1.8 %; −1.4 % until 2026-10-26, when its count's dips below 2000 drew the backgrounds twice) |
| D8 (an isolated square 16 × 16 and a strip 8 × 32, each centred on the solenoid's axis, 20 000 carriers, B ramping between ±0.25 in a triangle of period 20, `\|Ḃ\|` = 0.05, over t = 0–200, 2 time units skipped after each corner; the one electrode floating 40 away; `--ignored`, 5 min) | the power the induced field gives the carriers, `Σ q E·Δx/dt`, and the current through the left half, `Σ q Δy/dt`, per ramp signed by Ḃ | Ohm's law: `σ Ḃ² ∫ u dA` and `σ Ḃ ∫ u dy` along the middle line, u the rectangle's torsion function (Saint-Venant's series), σ as D1 | 5 % each | square: power −2.44 ± 1.21 %, current −3.31 ± 1.89 %; strip: −0.35 ± 1.72 %, −0.67 ± 1.95 % |
| D1 (resistor; `--ignored`, 100 s) | `σ = j/E` in a conductor 16 × 4 between two contacts at 3.2 (16 000 carriers, t = 8–136): the current from the moves, the field averaged over the middle half | as D1 | 5 % | −1.88 % (statistics 1.3 %; ohmic contacts emitting as a thermal wall: the bulk field V/L to 0.2 %. With carriers placed in the layer −0.89 %; with the space-charge-limited contacts −2.74 %, +0.12 % releasing at rest) |

D5 is static screening: the plates' field is screened within a Debye length, and the measured layer is that length to the statistics once the carriers' softening is well below it. At softening 0.05 (`h/λ_D` = 0.18, about the macroparticles' spacing) the screening came out longer: +13 % over t = 5–30, +8.5 % over 5–60; at 0.025 +1.0 % (+4.4 % over 5–60), at 0.0125 +4.9 %: converged. A level's preview has `h/λ_D` ≈ 0.16 (its verification half that): its screening layers are some 8 % too thick, within the verification's error bar. The first fit, unweighted in log space, let the noisy tail set the slope (+14 % at 0.05).

*Thermal noise.* D7 shows that the model's carriers, contacts and lattice are in equilibrium together: the resistor's current fluctuates as Nyquist's `4kT/R` (one-sided) with kT a macroparticle's. That is the noise of lines of charge, infinite along z, each moving as one, and so the model's own: a real conductor's carriers fluctuate independently along z, its voltage noise falls as `kT/C` with C growing as its length, and in a z-invariant world it vanishes. The noise the levels' metals show halves when the macroparticles are doubled (the verification's), so their goals average over windows long enough to keep it within the error bars (the floating contacts' ~0.1–0.3 at 100 macroparticles per area), and no level asks for a noise: the Johnson-noise level the plan sketched (docs/DRUDE.md) is not built, since no symmetry makes its goal real physics.

*Eddy currents.* A field along z that changes in time (`physics::tube::SolenoidWave`: a long solenoid around the board on a time-dependent supply) induces `E = −½ Ḃ ẑ × (x − x₀)` about the solenoid's axis (Faraday's law, `∇ × E = −Ḃ ẑ`; these units write the Lorentz force `q(E + v × B)`, so no 1/c), which pushes every particle; the step takes B and Ḃ at the positions' time (a field without a wave runs bit for bit as before). In an isolated conductor only its curl drives a steady current: the charges it moves to the surface cancel the rest, and Ohm's law gives the stream function `ψ = −σ Ḃ u`, u the conductor's torsion function (`∇²u = −1`, 0 on the edge), the power `σ Ḃ² ∫ u dA` (a strip `w` ≪ `b`: `σ Ḃ² w³ b/12`, so N laminations of the same metal take `1/N²` of it) and, for uniform Hall angle, no Hall term (`∇ × (ẑ × j) = ẑ ∇·j = 0`). The eddy currents' own magnetic field is left out, as everywhere in the tubes: valid where the skin depth is far larger than the conductor. D8 confirms the law on a square and on a strip 8 × 32 (so on laminations). Two limits were met on the way. At `Ḃ = 0.1` the square came out 5 % low at any amplitude (0.2 or 0.5): the induced field, 0.4 at its edges (`qEℓ/kT ≈ 0.2`), warms the carriers, as in the Ohm's law level; the linear response needs `qEℓ/kT ≲ 0.1`. And the scaled metal's charges settle slowly in elongated pieces: four strips 4 × 16 side by side (the square cut in four) each carry at every corner the uniform current of the induced field's uniform part across them (~5.4 in the outer ones), decaying over several time units (`1/(4πσN)` with the small depolarization factor N of a polarization along a long strip, slower with its neighbours); a continuum model (Ohm's law with the Hall tensor, diffusion, charge conservation, resolved Debye layers; `target/scratch/eddy_continuum*.py` of the session) shows the same slow mode when a nearby conductor couples the drive into it. *Grounding in an open z-invariant world* (docs/OPEN_PROBLEMS.md, OP-1, resolved): a grounded electrode with no enclosing conductor is held at 0 in the free-space gauge `φ = −2λ ln r`, whose zero lies at the unit radius, and can draw net charge from that "infinity"; with net charge the potential coefficients `−2 ln d` (negative beyond d = 1) leave the electrostatic energy unbounded below, and a large floating conductor next to such an electrode polarizes toward it while the electrode charges up, without end. A strip 8 × 32 with a small grounded electrode 1.5 below it did so with no field at all (the electrode's charge 0.7 → 228 over t = 5–60, a quarter of the carriers moving toward it); floating, or with a grounded box around everything, it stayed at 0 ± 0.04. The shipped levels' metals all sit between contacts held at potentials; a level with an isolated metal keeps its electrodes floating or encloses it in a grounded conductor. *An isolated metal's heat* (`TubeState::induced_work`): in a metal without contacts the heat goal measures the induced field's work alone. There the electrostatic field (the carriers' own, their surface charges') only stores and returns energy over a cycle, yet with the leapfrog's finite step it heats the carriers through their graininess: on Eddy currents' plate (12 × 12, no drive) the field's whole work came to 2.57 per time at the preview's resolution and 0.60 at the verification's (∝ dt²), against an eddy-current heat of 1.24; the induced field's work is exactly 0 without a drive (test `isolated_metal_heat_is_the_induced_work`, which also requires the heat quadratic in the drive: at B₀ = 0.4 and 0.8 on Eddy currents' plate the ratio 3.929, required within 10 % of 4). Driven at B₀ = 0.34 and 0.17 (ω = 0.25), the induced work came out 7.8 and 10.7 % below local Ohm's law in the verification, 11.6 and 17.6 % in the preview; at the preview's step with 2 and 4 times the macroparticles 1.158 and 1.149 against the preview's 1.093 and the verification's 1.140: converged in the macroparticles, about 7 % below Ohm's law at both drives (not the carriers' warming, which grows with the drive). D8 found 2.4 % on a square 32 mean free paths wide; this plate is 12: a kinetic correction near the walls, where the eddy current runs, is of that order (`ℓ/a`; not measured separately). At ω = 0.125 the eddy-current level's plate reads 5–7 % below Ohm's law. Off the solenoid's axis a piece's induced field has a uniform part, which its surface charge cancels; its work on the carriers' thermal motion makes the reading noisy (a plate 8 × 6 six below the axis scattered by ±20 % between layouts that left it alone, at the verification's resolution), so the levels centre their metals on the axis. *Cuts as capacitors*: a cut has no thickness, so the surface charges on its two faces lie a Debye length apart (0.4) and the cut carries current across as a capacitor, `1/(ωC)`; on a core 12 × 8 cut along its length (two strips 12 × 4) at ω = 0.25 it took 61 % more heat than Ohm's law gives (its cut's impedance comparable to a strip's resistance), at ω = 0.125 14 % (the excess dissipation ∝ ω²); a real lamination's coating is far thinner but a real metal conducts so well that its `ωε/σ` is ~1e-16. The eddy levels use ω = 0.125. *A cold electrode* (`TubeSpec::saturation` 0): the cathode emits nothing (its weight is left as the space-charge rule sets it; a saturation of 0 had divided it to 0). *Isolated metals*: a metal may have no contact only if every electrode floats (a fixed charge, no supply, drive or circuit: `setup_issues`; OP-1). *The field's work* (`TubeState::field_work`, per lattice): the push's exact energy account, `(q dt/2) E·(v_old + v_new)` over a lattice's carriers (the magnetic rotation does no work), is the Joule heat in a steady state with far less noise than the lattice's own take (each collision exchanges about kT at random; the field's work fluctuates `qEℓ/kT` times as much); a level's heat goal (`TubeSpec::heat_goals`) is a metal's mean of it over a window, verified as the current goals (the fine run's value, 1.5 times the runs' difference as its error).

The thresholds: 5 % is the target for the model's transport coefficients (the medium's own correction at this density, the field's nonlinearity and the statistics are each of order 1 %); the collision rate and the energy balance are exact in the model. Over six seeds of the carriers: D1 −2.25 % (spread 0.67 %), D4 1.27 % (0.50 %), D6 −0.91 % (0.31 %).

**In tube levels** (`level::tube::MetalSpec`, `TubeSpec::metals`): a metal is a rectangle on the board's grid with its contacts (box electrodes along its sides, each face within a side, so that a contact gives off carriers into the metal only), its carriers' density, `q/m` and thermal speed, its mean free path and core radius (the cores' density `1/(2aℓ)` follows) and its macroparticles per area (the verification's twice as many, at half the step). `setup_issues` refuses: cores denser than `a² n = 0.005` (the medium's correction beyond 3 %), a step that does not resolve the plasma oscillation (`ω_p dt ≤ 0.2`, `ω_p² = 4π ρ|q/m|`) or the mean free time (`4 σ_v dt ≤ ℓ`), a mean free path above a quarter of the metal's width (the carriers must scatter off the cores, not its walls), anything inside a metal (electrodes, other metals, projectiles, free charges), more than 15 000 carriers in the verification. A current goal on a contact counts its lead's current (`Carriers::Net`: what it takes in less what it gives off, `TubeState::released`). The maps add the backgrounds (the potential map as fixed line charges on the cells its carriers are binned on, so that a neutral metal reads neutral; the run's Maxwell field in its initial static solve and its charge density), and a carrier born at a contact is attributed to the nearest emitting electrode of its sign. A potential goal may be measured against another electrode (`PotentialGoal::reference`: a voltmeter across the two, the Hall voltage); a heat goal is a metal's mean heat (above, *the field's work*). The player's solenoid may follow a source in time (`TubeSpec::solenoid_drive`: its shape, the supply setting its level), with the induced field about the board's centre. *Cuts* (`ElementKind::Cut`, the player's): an insulating slit of any length and angle within one metal, drawn from one grid node to another (`Level::cut_between`: the node, the angle, the length; `cut_metal`: both ends in the metal's rectangle, on its edges too, its middle inside). Each is a wall inside its metal's lattice (`physics::lattice::Lattice::walls`): a carrier whose step's chord crosses one, from the step's start or from where it was last reflected, is reflected specularly off its line, position and velocity, in the same loop as off the metal's sides (up to 8 reflections a step); a carrier on a wall counts as on both sides, so it cannot slip through, and a cut that ends on the metal's edge reaches a tenth of a unit past it, so that none slips round its end. The slit has no thickness. Until 2026-10-20 a cut ran from edge to edge along x or y and split its metal into separate lattices (each piece at least four mean free paths wide); the owner asked for cuts of any length and angle. Tests: `cuts_follow_their_rules` (straight, oblique, partial cuts allowed; an end outside, along an edge, across two metals, no length, too many refused); `cuts_insulate`, Magnetoresistance at B = 0 and 7 V over t = 10–80: from edge to edge across the long strip, a straight cut takes its current from 0.7320 to 0.0000 and an oblique one ((10, 13) to (14, 17)) to 0.0080 (required below 3 %: no path is left), one stopping a unit short of the far edge leaves 0.527 (required above 5 %: a path remains), one along the short wide strip changes its current by −1.58 % (required within 10 %, each side of the slit carrying a conductor's surface charge). *Strong fields*: a metal's carriers may turn by at most a radian per step in the field (`|q/m| B dt ≤ 1`, `setup_issues`; the Boris rotation's angle `2 atan(ω_c dt/2)` is then within 8 % of the true one, the verification's half step within 2 %): the levels' metal (step 0.035) up to B ≈ 28. A run with metals keeps a display frame every few steps, within 4 million particle-frames (`FRAME_BUDGET`; the goals' currents are sampled every step).

The levels' metal (Ohm's law, Long and thin, Potentiometer, Hall probe, Positive carriers, Magnetoresistance, Strength and sign; `scripts/designs/common.py` `METAL`; Positive carriers' lower strip the same with `q/m = +1` over a negative background, both its strips at 50 macroparticles per area; Few carriers or many's upper strip, at 50, and its lower one with four times the carriers colliding four times as often, ρ = 8, ℓ = 0.25, a = 0.002 (`a/2ℓ = 0.004`), the same σ = ρτ, the level's step 0.019 (`ω_p dt = 0.19`)): ρ = 2, `q/m = −1`, `σ_v = 2` (kT/q = 4, small against the supplies' tens, so that the contacts' and walls' own voltages stay a few per cent), ℓ = 1, a = 0.01 (`a/2ℓ = 0.005`), step 0.035 (`ω_p dt = 0.18`), 50–100 macroparticles per area. Measured on a strip 16 × 4 between two contacts, the current into the far contact over t = 5–35: 0.908, 1.724, 2.428, 3.173, 3.839 at V = 8, 16, 24, 32, 40 and −2.614 at −24: in proportion to within a slow fall of I/V (0.1135 to 0.096) as the carriers warm in the field (`qEℓ/kT` 0.1 to 0.6); the bulk theory gives 0.1175 V (at the weakest field the contacts' boundary layers and the walls' surface charge, below, take 3 %). Over t = 10–40 the lattice takes 81.4 per time where the supply gives `IV` = 3.00 × 30 = 90.1 (Ohm's law level at 30; a strip 16 × 4 alone at 26.5: 62.8 of 69.7): 90 %. The rest, not measured separately, would be the energy the field gave the carriers since their last collision, brought into the contact they reach (they arrive warmer than the lattice); the Peltier heat moves between the two contacts, both thermal walls, and nets out. With the heat-sink contacts of rule (0) the lattice took 58.3 of 85.5 and the gap had been read as Peltier heat. The game shows the lattice's rate over the last 10 time units: each collision's heat fluctuates by about kT, and over one unit (12 000 collisions) the rate was uncertain by ±20. *The drift drawn* (`level::tube::metal_steps`): chevrons across each metal move with its carriers' mean displacement (the mean over the carriers in it at two consecutive frames, summed from the run's start, along the way they have gone), the drift of the carriers as a whole beside each one's thermal zigzag, drawn as strongly as it is fast against a tenth of the thermal speed (without a supply the drift is the thermal motion's noise, ~1 % of it, and the chevrons all but vanish). The few carriers traced with their paths stay as many: one a contact takes is replaced by another carrier of the metal, drawn in an order unrelated to place (a hash of identity). They are drawn from a sample recorded at every step (`level::tube::FINE_SAMPLE`, about 160 carriers: those whose identity is a multiple of `TubeRun::fine_every`; `TubeFrame::paths`), their trails over the last 0.8 time units along those steps, and between frames each moves along its steps (`TubeRun::frame_between`, `trail`): from the frames alone, 0.28 apart on Magnetoresistance (`FRAME_BUDGET`), a trail of 0.4 was two straight strokes, and a carrier's gyration in its field (B = 7: radius `σ_v/ω_c` ≈ 0.3, period 0.9, about three radians between collisions) did not show. Replaced by the newest carrier, just given off next to a contact and soon taken again, the traces gathered at the contacts: on Ohm's law's reference over t = 20–80, 22 % of them within half a unit of either, 4 % when drawn so (uniform: 6 %; the contacts thin the carriers next to them). Test `metal_drift_carries_the_current`: on Ohm's law's reference over t = 10–40 the chevrons move at 0.3816 along the strip against `I/(ρW)` = 3.002/(2 × 4) = 0.3752 (+1.7 %; required within 10 %: a step's mean leaves out the carriers a contact takes during it), 0.003 across. *What the model's metals resemble* (2026-10-20; game `scales.rs`, `resemblance`). A metal's own ratios, the same in any units, are its plasma oscillations per collision, ω_p τ, and its screening length per mean free path (Debye's here, ≈ 0.8/(ω_p τ)); everything else (ω_c τ, ωτ, qEℓ/kT, the size in mean free paths) is set by the level's fields, drive and shape. Against real conductors at 300 K (Drude: density, effective mass, mobility, permittivity; Thomas–Fermi screening for a metal's degenerate carriers), counting a material only where the level's strongest field read through its mobility (ω_c τ = μB) is a laboratory's (≤ 30 T), and naming the most common within ×3 of both ratios, else ×10: the levels' metal (ω_p τ 2.0, screening 0.40 ℓ) is n-type silicon at 10¹⁸ per cm³ without a field (×1.39: Ohm's law, Long and thin, Potentiometer, Laminations), at 10¹⁷ in Eddy currents' (×1.65), and n-type germanium at 10¹⁶ in the Hall levels' fields (×1.85; silicon would need 80 T); Eddy currents in silicon's (0.70, 1.14 ℓ) is n-type silicon at 10¹⁶ (×1.08). Real metals' ω_p τ is 50–500 (copper 401, aluminium 164, iron 49, stainless steel 6.7) and their screening 0.001–0.1 ℓ: the step must follow the plasma oscillation (ω_p dt ≤ 0.2) while a level runs for many collision times, so the steps grow as ω_p τ, and the model affords about 1 at playable cost (even stainless steel's 6.7 would make a level ten times slower). The model's metals are lightly to moderately doped semiconductors; a metal's behaviour where it only needs the regime (Ohm's law with ω_c τ, ωτ ≪ 1, as Eddy currents in silicon's) is reproduced, its own ratios are not. *A gated channel* (2026-10-20, level Gate): a gate beside a channel a few screening lengths thin empties it (depletion) or fills it from the contacts (accumulation), and the channel's current follows: the field effect, which a real metal's atom-thin screening forbids. A channel 8 × 2 of density 1 between gates 1.5 away: the drain current 0.26 at the gate 0, 0.18, 0.137, 0.07 at −10, −15, −25, pinch-off beyond −40. Its numerics: below threshold the current crosses the gate's barrier thermally, so it depends exponentially on the barrier, and the grainy field of coarse macroparticles rocks it: at 100 and 200 macroparticles per area the preview held excess carriers (at the gate 0 a current 45 % high; between −15 and −35 a false plateau), at 400 the preview, 800 and the verification agree within 10 % to about −25, and deeper (−46: 0.078, 0.052, 0.007 at 200, 400, 800) the subthreshold current converges only with still more. The level's goal lies where it converges; pinch-off is shown as it is. *The maps inside a metal* (the View's "current" and "heat", 2026-10-20; `physics::tube::CellAccounts`, `level::tube::TubeRun::metal_maps`). Bookkeeping only (no golden hash changed): every step, each lattice books by unit cell the field's work on its carriers there (their share of `field_work`, and the induced field's of `induced_work`) and the charge they carry through it, `q Δx` along each step's chord split at the cells' edges (`Lattice::book_flux`). Booked whole to the cell where each step began, the charge carried was biased at the walls: every edge between two rows gave its thermal crossings' overshoot to the row below and took it from the row above, which cancels inside and left the rows along the walls a spurious flux into them, ±0.136 per area on Magnetoresistance's long strip against its 0.19 along it (the crossing rate `ρσ_v/√(2π)` = 1.60 times the mean crossing step ≈ 1.25 σ_v dt = 0.088 gives 0.14); along the chords, 0.004 at most. The frames keep the accounts (as f32, so far: the heat goal's work, an isolated metal's the induced field's, `MetalView::isolated`), and the maps take their differences: a cell's current density and power per area over a span, averaged with the cells about it that no cut separates from it (3 × 3). The carriers' thermal motion makes every cell noisy (the Johnson–Nyquist noise of D7, cell by cell: a cell's ~50 macroparticles over 4 time units carry about a fifth of an ordinary drift): the span is cut into five batches whose spread gives each value's standard error, and the game fades a cell out as its value sinks into its noise (within one standard error gone, beyond three in full). Measured signal to noise per cell (median, 3 × 3, over 4 time units): Hall probe's reference 17 (power) and 26 (current); Eddy currents' 2.6 and 1.5; Laminations' about 1 at any span. Under a swinging field (`TubeRun::drive_period`) the maps take the whole half periods before the time shown (up to four; each a whole cycle of the heat, which goes as `Ḃ²`): the power as their mean, the current by lock-in, `J = ⟨j Ḃ⟩/⟨Ḃ²⟩` per cell times the rms of Ḃ (the rms of the current in step with the field's change; a plain mean cancels it), with signal to noise 9 (current) and 9.4 (power) on Eddy currents' reference and 6.9 and 5.6 on the uncut Laminations core. An isolated metal's heat map is the induced field's work: its total is the heat (the surface charges' field does no net work), its distribution differs from the dissipation `j²/σ` by `∇·(φ j)`, which moves energy within the metal (largest at the corners); the full field's work would be the dissipation's distribution plus the electrostatics' numerical heating (∝ dt², above), uniform and twice the eddy heat at the preview's resolution. Tests: `d9_cell_accounts_add_up` (an isolated plate 6 × 4, 400 steps: the cells' works against the lattice's and their charge carried against every carrier's own `Σ q Δx` booked independently, to 3.7e-16 and 3.3e-15 of their scale; required 1e-9) and `metal_maps_add_up`: (a) Eddy currents' heat map over the goal's window against the goal's measure, +0.096 % (required 1 %: the frames are interpolated at the window's ends); (b) Ohm's law's strip, `j_x` over its middle half times its width against the current into its end over t = 10–40, +0.44 % (required 3 %), and `j_y` within 1.2 % of `|j_x|` in every row (required 15 %; the start-cell booking gave ±70 %); (c) the eddy currents' circulation against their heat over t = 40–60, `L/P` −20.35 against Ohm's `−2⟨Ḃ⟩/⟨Ḃ²⟩` = −20.63 (−1.36 %; required 10 %: Ohmic, `ψ = −σḂu` with `u` the plate's torsion function, so `L = 2∫ψ = −2σḂ∫u` and `P = σḂ²∫u` whatever σ and the shape); (d) the lock-in maps over t = 24.5–125, `L/P` −23.17 against `−2/Ḃ_rms` = −23.82 (−2.74 %, required 10 %); (e) the error bars: on Ohm's law's uniform middle the cells scatter by 0.032 against a reported standard error of 0.042 (ratio 0.76, required 0.6–1.6). The maps show what the totals hid: Hall probe's floating side contacts carry about a fifth of the strip's current past the metal under them (2.43 and 2.56 of 3.1 in the two columns there; equipotentials, they take the carriers in at one end and give them back at the other), and charge through the strip until t ≈ 60 (from 0 V towards the strip's 14 V: 10.7 at t = 10, 13.7 at 80), so that over t = 10–80 the strip carries 3.04 before them and 3.15 after (3.10 and 3.11 over t = 80–160); in Magnetoresistance's long strip at B = 5 the current crowds towards the edge the magnetic push leaves (0.188 against 0.156 per area along the far one over t = 10–80, ~7 standard errors): consistent with the Ettingshausen effect (the faster carriers pushed harder across than the Hall field holds them, the slower and, with τ = ℓ/v, more mobile ones left behind), not examined further. *The energy account* (2026-10-20; §9). Each electrode books the kinetic energy of the carriers it takes in less that of those it gives off (`TubeState::contact_energy`). Test `d10_carriers_energy_account` (D1's resistor at 3.2): without cores (no collisions) the carriers' kinetic energy changes by the field's work less the contacts' net take to 1.5e-15 (required 1e-10): the push, the walls and the contacts lose nothing. With cores the account closes to first order in the step: a collision's heat is taken at the velocity of its moment within the step, the leapfrog stores the step's middle's, and they differ by the part of the step's kick between them, so each collision books `m (½ − φ) g·(u′ − v)` (zero mean; `g` mostly the carriers' own grainy field) and `m g² (½ − φ)²` (a small surplus): over 10 time units in 40 blocks the blocks' rms residual falls 3.35 times for a step 4 times shorter (required 2.5; ∝ dt: 4), and the run's residual is 8.9e-4 of the field's work at dt = 0.005 (required 5e-3; 8.1e-3 at 0.02). Where the circuit's energy goes (test `metal_energy_balance`, Ohm's law's reference over t = 10–40): the supply gives `IV` = 90.06 per time, the field works on the carriers at 86.09 (−4.4 %, required 5 %), the lattice takes 81.48 (90.5 % of `IV`), and D10's account carries the field's work's rest into the contacts with the carriers. The field's work falls short of `IV` systematically: 0.94–0.97 of it in windows over t = 10–80 in the preview, 0.91–0.95 at the verification's resolution. *The circuit's account closed* (2026-10-20; test `d11_circuit_energy_account`): a carrier is given off and taken in where the potential differs from its contact's (it is released in the layer its contact keeps, half a segment from the face, `EMISSION_OFFSET`; one coming back to its own contact is taken within three quarters of a segment), and each such step, `q (V − φ)` at a release and `q (φ − V)` at an absorption (φ the potential of everything else there; `TubeState::potential_steps`, booked on request: it sums the potential over every particle at each, 44 % more time in Ohm's law's preview), is circuit energy that the field's work on the carriers leaves out: `circuit = W + S − H`, with `H` the step's own remainder (the exact motion conserves the energy). In D1's resistor (4000 carriers, t = 5–25 in 8 blocks) with no current `H` is 1.84 ± 0.12 per time at dt 0.02 and 0.27 ± 0.06 at 0.005, 6.8 times less for a step 4 times shorter (required 3; first order: 4; with or without collisions alike: the leapfrog's work near the contacts), and at 3.2 V, the circuit giving 0.50, it is 1.82 ± 0.06, V = 0's within 0.1 standard errors (required 3): the steps carry the circuit's share exactly. On Ohm's law's reference (preview, t = 10–40 in 6 blocks; test `metal_energy_balance`) `S` is 39.6 per time (44.0 % of `IV`) and `H` 35.6 ± 1.4 (39.5 %); with the supply at 0, 36.1 and 32.0 ± 0.4, 2.5 standard errors apart (required 3; 3.6 ± 1.4, so a part of `H` of up to ~4 % of `IV` may grow with the current). Both are mostly the carriers' exchange with the contacts' layers, which goes on without a current, so the gap `W − IV` (−4.0) is the field's work on the carriers with no current at all (−4.1 per time at the preview's step; in D1's resistor +0.52 at dt 0.02 and +0.81 at 0.005: the contacts' layers trade energy with the carriers at rest, and how much depends on the step). At the verification's resolution `S` is 26.7 (29.7 %) and `H` 20.6 (22.9 %). The heat goals' metals are isolated (Eddy currents, Eddy currents in silicon, Laminations): no contact, no such gap. *The Hall effect drawn* (`TubeRun::hall_forces`): at each metal's middle in a field, a free-body diagram of its average carrier, every arrow drawn from it and labelled on the board: the carriers' drift (their mean displacement over the last time unit in the middle; its direction, thin), the magnetic push on it `q⟨v⟩ × B`, the electric push `q⟨E⟩` (the mean field over the middle: the central three fifths along it, from a unit inside each side, whose surface charge holds its potential against its surroundings, whose fields across it differ between the sides; `TubeRun::middle_field`, recorded by the run at every frame, `TubeRun::middle`, and averaged over the drift's time unit) and the collisions' mean push, which holds the drift steady: the carriers' mean momentum stays put, so it is minus the other two (the three close). In a long strip the electric push's part across cancels the magnetic push and the drift runs straight along; in Magnetoresistance's short wide strip the contacts carry the piled charge off and the drift turns toward the Hall angle. Until 2026-10-19 the diagram drew only the field's part across the strip's long side, with the two pushes hung off the drift's arrow: in the short wide strip, whose current runs across its long side, that part was the supply's driving field. The carriers' graininess scatters the field across from frame to frame: on Hall probe's reference by 0.11 against its 0.47 (on the centre line alone, as first drawn, 0.19), on Positive carriers' weakly driven lower strip by 0.20 against 0.27 (0.35); over a time unit (about three frames) still by ~40 % there. The field the arrows and the field map's arrows take from a frame (`TubeRun::field_at`, `e_at`) softens each carrier as a uniformly charged rod of radius 0.5 (the preview's segment), exactly Coulomb's beyond it, like the physics' thinner rods. Softened as `r⃗/(r² + ε²)` (ε = 0.5), the carriers' field never became Coulomb's and failed to cancel their background's exact one by `2λε²/r³` each: from Positive carriers' upper strip (128 charges) a field across the lower strip, `πε²ρ(1/h₁ − 1/h₂)` for strips long against their distance `h`, 0.56 between its floating contacts by that estimate and 0.60 measured (the maps' potential inside them against theirs), which took the lower strip's electric push to 1.73 of its magnetic one. (The potential map, on the GPU, softens the backgrounds alike and was not affected.) Test `hall_forces_balance`: on Hall probe's reference and on both strips of Positive carriers', over t = 20–60 (every 2), the electric push across the strip opposes the magnetic one at every sample and their sums are in the ratio 1.049, 0.957 and 1.300 (1.288 before the sampled carriers moved along their steps between frames, which shifts the drift measured between interpolated frames; required 0.85–1.5: the model's Hall factor lies between 1 and 4/π, D4, whatever the carriers' sign; a ratio of sums, as a mean of ratios leans high when the divisor scatters). The lower strip's ratio follows its drive, not its carriers' sign: driven at −30 like the upper one, it gives 1.043 beside the upper's 0.975 (t = 20–80); at its reference's 12 its carriers are pushed 2.5 times more weakly (`qEℓ/kT` 0.19 against 0.47), nearer the weak field's 4/π (how much the warming of the carriers lowers the Hall factor was not measured separately). The charge piles up within the metal's dielectric relaxation time `1/(4πσ)` ≈ 0.2, before the drift can be measured. The Hall voltage (16 × 4, floating side contacts at mid-length, V = 30, I ≈ 3.15): −0.90, −1.44, −1.68 at B = 0.9, 1.2, 1.5 and +0.22 at B = 0 (with the heat-sink contacts −0.58, −1.15, −1.44, −2.1, −2.33 at B = 0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.2, 1.6), the floating contacts' thermal noise ~0.1–0.3 with 100 macroparticles per area (the Hall probe averages over t = 10–80): below the weak-field `R_H I B` as `ω_c τ = 0.4 B` grows (the Hall factor falls towards 1, D4) and as the current contacts short the strip's ends.

*The walls: a conductor's surface charge.* The carriers' density is 12 % low within half a unit of a strip's side walls in the Ohm's law level (transverse fields ±1.3 there). It is not the softening (softened carriers against an exact background shift the potential by `1.5π ρ h²/2` ≈ 0.02 at a wall, 0.4 % of kT/q): it is the charge a conductor carries at its surface, set by its potential against its surroundings (the grounded envelope), here spread over the Debye length (0.4) instead of an atomic layer. With the strip's two contacts held at one potential (no current), the density in bands 0.25 wide across it: uniform (2.0 ± 0.03) at 0; 1.58 at the walls, recovering over ~0.75, at +20; 2.40 at the walls at −20. The deficit at +20, 0.21 per unit length of wall, is the 4π σ ≈ 2.6 of the field outside (Gauss). It takes a few per cent of a strip's conductance (more where its potential is far from the surroundings') and cancels in a Hall voltage (symmetric). An earlier version of this section called it a softening artefact.

**Measured corrections** (seed averages):
- *The medium's density.* A Lorentz gas of fixed cores is the Boltzmann equation's random medium only in the dilute limit: D6 at `a² n_ion` = 0.00125 / 0.0025 / 0.005 gives −0.9 / −1.8 / −2.7 %, close to `1.1 a²n ln(a²n)` (the 2D Lorentz gas's logarithmic density correction). Denser media deviate more (D1's measure at `qEℓ/kT = 0.15`: −8.6 % at 0.01, −13 % at 0.02). Levels keep `a² n_ion ≤ 0.005`.
- *The field.* The carriers warm by the field between collisions, which lowers σ: −1.3 % at `qEℓ/kT = 0.1` (D1 against D6 over the seeds; the Monte Carlo without geometry: −1.7 ± 0.5 %).
- *The time step.* With the moves measured, dt = 0.02 and 0.005 agree to the statistics (1 %).

*The backgrounds counted twice below 2000 particles* (found 2026-10-26 by the Darwin stage's energy test, Y25; fixed). The step sums the particles' fields by trees from `TREE_MIN` = 2000 particles and directly below; the direct sums (`Tube::field`) included the lattices' backgrounds, and the push added them again, so below 2000 particles the ions pulled twice as hard (from 28ed8b1, the metals' groundwork). The direct sums now leave the backgrounds out (`charges_field`) as the trees do, and one function adds the backgrounds and the induced field for the push and the display alike (`Tube::with_external`); test `trees_and_direct_sums_agree` holds the two paths to the trees' truncation for the same state (1e-6 of the largest field required, from `tree2d`'s 5e-7 a node; 1.2e-8 measured), where the bug had put the backgrounds' whole field between them. Every shipped level starts with at least 3200 carriers (Ohm's law and Ringing tank in the preview), but Inverter's contacts empty its metals while its gate is off, down to 457 particles in the preview (t ≈ 84) and 1022 in the verification (t ≈ 71): its runs changed in those phases and its golden hash with them (b75bba4cd48a338d → 6686e59e7055b324; every other level's held, the closest Amplifier at 2545). Its reference still solves: the drain 24.970 ± 0.004 while off (was 24.47 ± 0.59: the channel, no longer pulled by twice its ions, leaks less) and 17.45 ± 0.22 while on (17.45 ± 0.46), the goals 22.5–25.5 and 15–19; its gate solves on −15.8 to −3.4 (15.6 % of its ±40, was −14 to −4); the Drude tests run 4000 or more, D7 2000 (its contacts take and give, so its count dipped below in some steps: rerun after the fix, 0.0 % against −1.4 % before). A collisionless disc of 1500 carriers, closed, lost or gained ±120 of its total energy of ~870 within a plasma period at any step (0.02 to 0.0025: the forces were not the gradient of the energy); after the fix it keeps it within ±0.01 (the leapfrog's).

## 2.12 The carriers' own magnetic field (the Darwin limit) — *in progress* (`crates/physics/src/darwin2d.rs`, tests V-D1–V-D6; docs/DARWIN.md)

The tube world's charges make no magnetic field of their own yet. This stage adds it in the magnetoquasistatic (Darwin) limit, valid while the system is small against a wavelength and the speeds small against c (the owner, 2026-10-26; docs/DECISIONS.md, DEC-13). In the z-invariant world a current in the plane makes a field along z: `B_z = −∇²χ`, `A_T = ∇ × χẑ` (Coulomb gauge), `μ₀ = 4π/c²` (the game's Coulomb constant 1). With canonical momenta `P = mv + qA_T` the transverse field follows from the charges' canonical current `J_P = Σ qP/m` and response `β = Σ q²/m` (per area) by minimising `∫ B_z²/(2μ₀) + β|A|²/2 − J_P·A`: `(1/μ₀)∇⁴χ − ∇·(β∇χ) = (∇ × J_P)_z`. The induced field is implicit (no time derivative of the current), stable for a metal larger than its skin depth `1/√(μ₀β)` (explicit Darwin is not: docs/DARWIN.md §2.1).

*Method.* A staggered grid: `χ` and `B_z = Lχ` (`L = −∇²`, five-point) on the nodes, `A_x`, `A_y` and the deposited currents and `β` on the edges, so that `∫ J·A` pairs exactly and a loop of current is exactly the curl of a step in the stream function. A frame of nodes at `χ = 0`, `B_z = 0` (sine modes: flux escapes as from the infinite plane). Conjugate gradients on the symmetric positive operator `(1/μ₀)L² + curlᵀβ curl`, preconditioned by the vacuum's inverse `μ₀L⁻²` (exact by type-I sine transforms through a radix-2 FFT of our own: deterministic, no library planner choosing algorithms by the CPU), warm-started from the last solution.

*Tests* (thresholds set from the target accuracy before measuring):

| Test | Setup | Reference | Required | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| V-D1 | a square loop of sheet current K (63 × 63 nodes, h 0.25) | `B_z = μ₀K` inside, 0 outside | 1e-9 | to the solver's tolerance (exact on this grid) |
| V-D2 | the same loop's energy | `½LK²`, `L = μ₀` × the area | 1e-9 | to the solver's tolerance |
| V-D3 | nested loops | `∫J₂·A₁ = MK₁K₂`, `M = μ₀` × the inner area | 1e-9 | to the solver's tolerance |
| V-D4 | a disc of carriers (skin depth λ = 2, radius 6) in a round loop | London's `B₀ I₀(r/λ)/I₀(a/λ)` (a cylinder in an axial field), and `B₀` between disc and loop | 2 % (the grid's `O(h²/λ²)` and the disc's staircase edge) | 0.61 % worst, in 9 iterations |

A slab that ends was the first V-D4 and failed (the field at its faces 0.4 B₀): its screening current `−βA` had nowhere to close, as nothing in the field solver alone conserves charge (in the full model the electrostatic field closes it). A round disc closes it by symmetry.

*In the tube engine* (`Tube::darwin`, `physics/src/tube/darwin.rs`; tests in `crates/physics/tests/darwin.rs`). Each particle keeps `u = v + (q/m)A` (its canonical momentum per mass). A step: the push as before (the electrostatic field, the external solenoid by Boris) gives each velocity its impulse, which `u` takes too; the canonical current `Σ q u` and the response `Σ q²/m` are deposited on the edges with the interpolation's weights; the field is solved; `v = u − (q/m)A(x)`, and the canonical force's kick `(q/m) dt ∇(v·A)` from that solution at the same positions (the exact gradient of the interpolation, `grad_dot`) goes to both; the particles move. Until 2026-10-26 the kick came first, from the last step's solution: solved where the particles had been a move before, each one's own vector potential peaked behind it and dragged it, a numerical friction growing with the step and with the grid's sharpness (a collisionless closed disc lost 0.35 of its energy per time unit at dt 0.02, h 0.25, and 0.77 at h 0.125; 0.18 and 0.21 after; Y25 below). Collisions and walls change `u` by what they did to `v`; a particle given off starts with `u = v + (q/m)A`. Without a speed of light every path is as before, bit for bit. The velocity update thus carries the self-field's whole Lorentz force, `−∂A/∂t`, `∇(v·A)` and the change of `A` along the path (`v × B`), the induced part implicit. Split from the collisions, the field is solved from the velocities before them: in a steady current the drift then sits `dt/τ` above the velocities after a step (V-D5's first measurement: the field in the hole 9.5 % above `μ₀` times the arm's post-collision current at `dt/τ` = 0.04), a first-order error of the splitting, `dt/2τ` against the transport current.

*Holes* (`Lattice::with_hole`): a rectangle inside a metal with no cores, carriers or background, its edges walls: a ring, the inductor's turn. *Round metals* (`Lattice::with_disc`; docs/DECISIONS.md, DEC-14): the outline a regular polygon of 96 walls with the disc's area (its vertices 0.036 % beyond the radius, its sides 0.03 % within), the cores and carriers inside it, the background the uniform disc's in closed form (`lattice::disc_integrals`: outside `φ = −πR² ln r²`, inside `E = 2πr`; against the disc summed from 2000 exact rectangles across it 7.7e-6, required 1e-5, test `disc_background_is_the_disc`; `disc_lattice_stays_inside`). A carrier drawn into a core across a hole's edge or the outline is put on the core's inner side (it went outward, into the hole or out of the metal).

| Test | Setup | Reference | Required | Measured |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| V-D5 | a thin ring 12 × 12, hole 10 × 10 (wall 1, two mean free paths, against a skin depth ≈ 2: a lumped inductor), 2444 carriers, in a field `0.125 sin(0.8 t)` (an EMF of amplitude ~12); the speed of light 3 against 10⁴ (no effect); lock-in over 8 periods after one, two seeds (`--ignored`, 4 min) | the current, `B/μ₀` in the hole, lags the EMF by `tan φ = ωL_tot/R`: `(tan φ_self − tan φ_free)/ω = L/R` (the free run's φ the carriers' inertia), against `L = 2ΔW/⟨I²⟩` (the magnetic energy less the undriven twin's thermal part) and `R = P/⟨I²⟩` (the external field's work), `⟨I²⟩` the driven current's `\|I_ω\|²/2`; the currents' magnitudes in the ratio of their phases' cosines; R the same with and without the self-field | 15 % for L/R (each phase ~0.03 rad, L's energy ~5 %); 5 % for the magnitudes; 15 % for R (first 5 %, set without estimating R's noise: each run's ±6–9 %, the seeds' ratio ±7 %) | L/R 0.846 from the phases against 0.848 (−0.3 %; single seeds 0.81 and 0.88 against 0.79 and 0.91); L = μ₀ × 111–114 (between the hole's 100 and the outer 144); R 175–198 with the self-field, 167–182 without (ratio 1.071; the Drude estimate `perimeter/σw` ≈ 187); magnitudes 1.030 |

V-D5 was first a ring with a wall of 3 in a field rising at 0.1, its rise time `∫(I∞ − I)dt/I∞` against L/R: over three seeds 2.095 against 2.125 (−1.4 %), until the kick moved after the solve; then 1.80, and over nine seeds 1.18 against 2.00 with single seeds 0.13–2.88. The integral to t = 16 gathers the ring's thermal current long after the rise (τ ≈ 2): a seed's run moved by ~±1 whatever the scheme (even the free run's I∞ moved by 3 % between the kick's two orders, its own field negligible: the runs are chaotic), so neither order could be told from the other, and the first pass was within that noise. The impedance at one frequency by lock-in rejects that noise: the thick ring gave L/R within 6.5 % (the new order) and 7.8 % (the old), its R 13–17 % higher with the self-field either way, which the thin ring traced to the measure: the raw `⟨I²⟩` held the thermal current (`kT/L_tot` by equipartition, 40 % of the driven one without the self-field, where `L_tot` is the inertia alone), halving with twice the carriers. Measuring it took three tries before that. The current counted from one arm's carriers (~1500 drifting at 0.05 against a thermal speed of 1) scattered by ~25 % a sample, and their velocities after a step lie `dt/τ` below the drift the field was solved from; the ring's current read from the field in the hole, `B/μ₀`, sums every carrier round it.
